ARKLOW RALLY: Bijlsma Trader 4500
 multipurpose

Shipbuilder:.. .. Shipyard Bijlsma

HIS is the first delivery from a new series of multi-
 purpose cargo vessels developed by Conoship
Group shipbuilder Bijlsma, and the first of thtee sister ships enteting the expanding fleet of Irish short-sea operator Arklow Shipping. The design demonstrates the resourcefulness of North Netherlands shipbuilders in general, as they overcome the festtictions on newbuilding size imposed by the locks and canals of the local waterway systems. For Bijlsma, this involves using its inland shipyard at Wartena to fabricate large sub- assemblies which are then taken by barge to the covered dock at Lemmer for erection. Other steel structures are ptefabricated by subcontractors and brought to
Lemmer, with major outfit items, such as electrical work and machinery installation, also handled by outside specialists.

Arklow Rally is designed for the carriage of general and bulk cargoes, and features a longitudinally framed bottom structure under the cargo spaces, designed to sustain maximum catgo loadings of 15tonnes /m? and the cattiage of 25tonne steel coils. Strengthening for grab discharge and loading aground is also included. The single hold measures 63.00m x 11.70m and is closed by 10 steel pontoon covers, handled by a travelling gantry crane mounted on the hatch coaming, This is also used to move two 11.65m x 8.30m portable cargo divisions which can be fitted into any of seven positions in the hold.

The double hull structure contains separate wing and bottom tanks, with the latter divided by a centreline duct keel. These are arranged to carry water ballast and fuel, 4
 cargo vessel
 with additional water ballast capacity available in a forward deep tank, and the fore and aft peak tanks. Poop and forecastle erections surmount the main deck, with the former, and a two-tier deckhouse, providing accommodation for seven persons. Above this, a full width wheelhouse is equipped with a complete navigational aids and communication package. Provision for the carriage of dangerous goods includes fitting explosion-proof fans in the hold, and separate CO, fire extinguishing systems for holds and engineroom. An
A-60 bulkhead is also fitted between the engineroom and cargo hold.

The main engine is a Mak 6M25 unit having an MCR of 1800kW at 750rev/min, driving a CP propeller at 160rev/min through an Lohmann & Stolterfoht (today
Flender) reduction gearbox fitted with a power take-off to a 312kW alternator. A 150kW diesel-alternator set is also fitted. Main and auxiliary machinery is cooled by a fresh water system including heat exchangers and box coolers. Manoeuvting is accomplished using a
Barkemeyer free-hanging, flap-type spade rudder controlled by Rolls-Royce steering geat, and assisted by a 250kW bow thruster.

ASEAN EXPLORER: new-generation
 cable ship

Shipbuilder: 0000. Jurong Shipyard Pte Ltd, Singapore
Vessel’S NAME...  s Asean Explorer [0] 110100] 0\~| Grrr 1064
Owner/operator  Asean Cableship Pte Ltd, Singapore
D@SIQNES:  BMT Shipdesign Ltd, UK
FES tens Metter’ wiadnitaanet wagmectadans: sana Singapore
Total number of sister ships already COMpleted:  ee  ns Nil

SEAN Explorer has been designed to perform
 the duties of laying, burial, and maintenance of
 both conventional and lightweight cables, and can carry 6000tonnes of cable in three 15.50m diameter tanks, as well as storing 150 repeaters. In common with the latest cable ship designs, the vessel is laid out for over-stern operation, using four sheaves built into the deck: cable laying, tow wire, cable repair, and umbilical. The upper deck serves as the working deck, and is covered for most of its length by an extended forecastle, on to which the main superstructure, given over almost entirely to accommodation for 70 persons, has been built.

The cable deck is fitted with two lines, with the port side (main laying line) serviced by a 50tonne cable drum, 20tonne linear cable engine (LCE), a 6tonne/six-wheel pair draw-off/hold-back (DOHB) winch, a cable diverter, and an emergency cable brake. A second 50tonne cable drum, a 6tonne/four- wheel pair DOHB winch, and a cable diverter, service the starboard line, which is designed for cable recovery and repair. Two portable LCEs complete the handling package, which is electrically driven, and was supplied by the UK expert in this field, Dowty.

A 65tonne cable burial plough and associated equipment, including a 65tonne long-reach A-frame, an 80tonne tow winch, ROV, buoy-handling
 equipment, and an umbilical winch are also carried.
The cable equipment is controlled from a central position at the aft end of the cable deck, starboard side, with monitoring assisted by CCTV, whilst the main operations room is located in the aft end of the bridge.

The diesel-electric power arrangements utilise four
Siemens 3240kW alternator sets, each driven by
Wartsila 8R32 engines. These supply the vessel's total requirements, including the main propulsion units, comprising two Kamewa Aquamaster 3800kW azimuth thrusters, fitted at the stern, and three 2400kW tunnel thrusters, from the same manufacturer, installed forward. Together these give the vessel a dynamic positioning capability (DP2) which is controlled by an Alstom A-Series model
ADP21 Duplex DP. This allows the vessel to remain on station in conditions up to sea state 6.

Control, monitoring and main machinery alarms, ate masterminded by a Kongsberg Norcontrol
Datachief C20 vessel management system, with the same supplier contributing a Bridgeline 10 integrated bridge system to aid navigation and simplify bridge
Operations.

SG MRENG® willingness to master technological challenges has led the company to a pioneering position - together with competitor Hyundai - in the construction of floating production, storage and offloading (FPSO) units, which utilise its shipbuilding and engineering abilities to the full. Completed by
Samsung in June 2002, Bonga will be fitted out in the
UK, during 2003, with specialist equipment necessary to process 225,000barrels/day of stabilised crude oil at a maximum temperature of 60°C, and to offload to a tanker at a continuous rate of 7000m*/h - equivalent to 1million barrels /24h.

This work will be carried out at the Bonga oilfield, Nigeria, where the vessel will be permanently spread- moored in waters with depths ranging from 1000m to 1125m, using four groups of three mooring legs. The latter are located port and starboard/forward and aft, in accordance with a system devised after conducting a mooring analysis.

Bonga is double-hulled and has a 5 x 3 configuration of cargo tanks, and five pairs of wing water ballast tanks, two slop tanks, two diesel oil tanks, and two methanol tanks. Cofferdams are installed to separate the latter from other compartments. The machinery space is located right aft under the accommodation spaces, and is fitted with a double bottom laid out as sludge tanks. The topside processing facilities will be installed on a deck elevated 5.50m above the main deck, and a helideck is fitted above the accommodation.

The hull structure was developed after investigation of static and dynamic loading using finite-element
 10

BONGA: Samsung leading the way with
FPSO construction
 analysis (FEA), and, specifically, this will allow the vessel to remain on station for 20years without drydocking. Longitudinal strength whilst under tow has also been the subject of special consideration, as have loadings caused by cargo 'sloshing' in the tanks.

With no independent means of propulsion, the machinery room is equipped mainly with auxiliary machinery, including two 1900kW/1000rev/min
Wartsila/ABB alternators. Cargo handling utilises 15
Framo hydraulically operated 1500m°/h pumps. A complement of 70 is carried, and lifesaving equipment comprises five 50-person totally enclosed lifeboats with watet-spray protection.

BRITISH TRADER: advanced LNG tanker

SNIPDUIICEr wee eee eee Samsung Heavy

Industries Co Ltd, Korea

Vessel'’S NAME!   ees British Trader

Hull NUMDEF     sss ssnneeenes 1380

Owner/operatOr:  eee BP Shipping, UK

DESIGNED...   eens Samsung Heavy

Industries Co Ltd, Korea

Model test establishment used:  SSPA, 
Sweden
 0 ree Isle of Man
Total number of sister ships
 already Completed: s s Nil
Total number of sister ships
 11] ke) 01 @) £0 (=) Ge 2

RITISH TRADER has been constructed in
 accotdance with the Gaz Transport & Technigas
Mark TT cargo containment system for the carriage of
LNG cargoes at ctyogenic temperature (-163°C) and atmospheric pressure. Within a double hull, which has been designed to last for a period of not less than 40 years, there are four cargo tanks, encased in a primary membrane of 1.2mm-thick corrugated, SUS 304L grade, stainless steel plates, and a secondary membrane of triplex glass wool. Insulation is made up of 270mm reinforced polyurethane foam, and the system allows the daily boil-off gas rate to be restricted to 0.15% of the total cargo volume.

Two 1700m’/h cargo pumps ate fitted in each tank, capable of completely discharging the cargo in 12 hours, following pre-cooling by LNG spray. Two 8000m’/h, low-duty, and two 26,000m’ high-duty compressors are installed, together with four spray pumps: a 550m’/h emergency cargo pump, two watming-up heaters, an LNG vaporiser, forcing vaporiser, a 14,000Nm°/h inert-gas generator and two 90Nm*/h nitrogen generators.

The machinery installation is centred on a Kawasaki ctoss-compound steam turbine, supplied with steam from boilers which burn fuel oil or boil-off gas, or a combination of both. This drives a six-bladed propeller for a service speed of 20.10knots. Electtical power is produced by two 3450kW steam-turbine driven alternators, with a similar-sized diesel set as stand-by. A 2500kW thruster is fitted at the bow.

British Trader is equipped with a 'centralised administration and control centre’, with integrated automation system (IAS) and shipboard management
 12
 system (SMS), which serves as a central command station for carrying out cargo and ballasting operations, and monitoring and remotely controlling the propulsion plant. Also installed is a one-man bridge system complying with Lloyd’s Registet’s NAV1/IBS notation, an ECDIS, a manoeuvring simulator, GMDSS console, voyage data recorder, and automatic identification system.

Other features of note included in the specification ate a ballast water exchange system which uses sequential empty/refill procedures to complete the opetation in under 30hours. The diesel-alternator engine has a low NOx emission, and there is an emergency operation for water ballast leakage, a CO, blowing agent for the insulation system, and all paint complies with environmental requirements.

CARLOS FISCHER: Kleven extends its fruit juice expertise

Shipbuilder: 00. Kleven Flore A/S, Norway (hull built at Daewoo-Mangalia, Romania)
VeSSEl'S NAMEL  cc  t s Carlos Fischer
Hull number:  147 (Daewoo number 5001)
Owner  Maritime Services Aleuropa GmbH, Germany
Designer.:  Kleven Flora Consult A/S, Norway
Model test establishment used:  Marintek, Norway 0 re Liberia
Total number of sister ships

AARLOS FISCHER is a larger version of two
 similar ships built by Kleven in 1993/1994 (Ouro
Do Brasil: Significant Ships of 1993), designed as a double-hull, four-hold refrigerated cargo ship - not a tanker - with each of the holds fitted with four (total 16), free-standing, stainless steel, cylindrical fruit juice tanks carrying either fresh orange juice at O°C orf concentrate at -10°C. Temperatures are maintained using a York refrigeration plant with three compressors, housed in a deckhouse on the upper deck, above No3 hold.

The system uses ammonia in the primary circuit and brine in the secondary, with the latter circulating through heat exchangers in each hold. Cooled air is then blown through ducts to all parts of the holds, which are insulated with polyurethane foam slabs sheathed with galvanised steel on the back, and polished aluminium facing the hold. For redundancy purposes, each of the compressors can carry 60% of the total loading, and a third main generator set with independent fuel supply, is fitted in the deckhouse.

As the vessel was too large for Kleven's building dock at Floro, her hull was built in Romania and towed to
Norway for completion, following which she has joined her owner's trade, loading orange juice in Brazil for processing in USA, Japan, and Europe, and mainly returning in ballast. For this reason, a large ballast capacity is provided, however, a revenue-earning 'back- haul’ facility has been included in the design, with arrangements for stowing 300TEU on the upper deck, including 75 freezer connections. Because of theit different characteristics, fresh juice and concentrate are handled through different pumps and pumping systems, all parts of which need to be constructed and maintained to a very high standard of cleanliness and finish.

A Sulzer 7RTA84C main engine supplied from
Wartsila, Italy, powers Carlos Fischer, developing 28,348kW at 102rev/min for a service speed of 21knots, when running at 94% MCR with a 5% sea margin. Also taken into account in the powering equation is a nominal 1200kW output from a 2000kW
ABB alternator, driven from a power take-off to a
Flender gearbox, and which supplies the refrigeration plant at sea. Two Wartsila/ABB 1299kW diesel- alternator sets are arranged aft of the main engine, with a third, similar unit, fitted in the refrigerating machinery room, as already noted. A Becker flap rudder is fitted aft
 14
 behind a flat-form hull running up from the base line, and incorporating a skeg. A 1500kW tunnel thruster is fitted forward.

Shipbuilders... eee  s Hyundai Mipo

Dockyard (HMD), Korea

VESSEl'S NAMEL neees Cimbria

FAI] MUMDES:... ee c ec  uns H 0036

Owner/Operator... Ahrenkiel

Shipmanagement GmbH
 & Co KG, Germany

Designer  Hyundai Mipo Dockyard, Korea

Model test

Research Institute, Korea
 scl 0 re Liberia
Total number of sister ships
 already COMpPIeted) sess nsstaees 1
Total number of sister ships

Still ON OFCOM...  s ueeeueunnans 6

NUMBER of 'firsts' apply to this new series of
 container ships. Notably, it marks the entry of
HMD into this segment of merchant shipbuilding.
Founded in 1976 as a major repair facility, the company moved into new construction in 1996, at a time of difficulty in securing repair and conversion contracts. A wish to add box-ships to the portfolio coincided with a decision by Ahrenkiel and Suisse-Atlantique, both better known as tramp-ship owners, to also enter this market for the first time, and, taking advantage of
HMD's berth availability, they placed the first of what became eight orders, two years ago, with the shipyard ptoducing a design from scratch, based on the owners' high-class specification.

The concept has been produced to conform not only with current regulations, but also encompasses requirements aimed at keels laid down after July 2002, patticularly those relating to AIS (automatic identification systems), VDR (voyage data recorders), and tin-free antifouling. Within a double-skin hull, there are six catgo holds forward of the engineroom and accommodation block, closed by 12 sets of pontoon hatch covers, with No 1 hold accessed through an extended forecastle, where a breakwater is fitted. Space on the upper deck aft of the engineroom can stow one
FRU and two TEU container stacks. A particular requirement was for the maximum possible intake of 14tonne homogeneously loaded containers, and by careful arrangement 2029TEU can be carried, with stability monitored by computet.

Looking for maximum speed with low fuel consumption, the designers selected a Hyundai-MAN
B&W 7K80MC-C. Mk 6 main engine developing 34,300bhp at 104rev/min, and driving a five-bladed propeller for a service speed of 24knots at a fuel consumption of 95tonnes/day. The electrical consumption, boosted by the requirements of 554 reefer containers (62 of which ate carried in No 6 hold), is satisfied by the output from four MAN B&W-driven 1500kW diesel-alternator sets, which also power the 1100kW bow thruster.

CMA CGM CLAUDEL: container ship from re-vamped Korean shipyard

Shipbuilder:  STX Shipbuilding Co Ltd, Korea
Vessel'S NAM@)  ees CMA CGM Claudel
Hull NUMDES     s ssssees naes S-1069
Owner/operator  EF Shipping Co, Gr 
Designer  STX Shipbuilding Co Ltd, Korea le Cyprus

Model test establishment used:.. KRISO, Korea
Total number of sister ships
 already completed: s nes 3
Total number of sister ships <1] |e) a 0) (0 [=] Senne 1

FTER a long period of financial uncertainty, the
 former Daedong shipbuilding company, operating from its modern, medium-sized, 'greenfield' site at
Chinhae, Korea, has emerged under new ownership and a new name, STX Shipbuilding Co (part of the
Ssangyong group), to carry on what was, despite its other problems, the successful technical business of its predecessor. These five ordets, placed by two Greek ownets, ate spearheading a planned diversion by the yard into containership building, and complementing a portfolio of product-tanker designs inherited from
Daedong. Trading on charter to a French operator, the ships conform to a layout conventional for this class of vessel, with a double hull surrounding a five-hold catgo space, loaded through 10 hatches closed by lift- away pontoon covers.

With no upper deck longitudinal girder to obstruct the stowage arrangement, 10 rows of containers can be loaded in the holds. The number of tiers carried vaties according to the heights of the units, and consequently the quoted total container intake of 1664TEU on deck, 938 in the holds (where 40ft cell guides are fitted) are maximum values. Total capacity is given as 2602TRU, but when homogeneously loaded to 14tonnes, this figure is reduced to 1856TEU.
Dangerous goods of most IMDG classes can be stowed in all holds, and 440 reefer plugs are available, although dangerous goods and teftigerated cargoes cannot be mixed. Four sets of Liebherr 45/40tonne swl single-jib cranes are fitted on the centreline: three sets forward of the superstructure, and one at the aft end which serves the open deck container stowage atea, above the machinery space.

The propulsion machinery consists of a Hyundai-
MAN B&W 8S70MC-C main engine producing 24,880kW at 91rev/min and driving a FP propeller for a fully loaded service speed of 22.30knots at 90%
MCR. Four 1400kW diesel-alternator sets are fitted, and there is a 1000kW bow thruster installed. A systematic study with CFD analysis and model testing has developed a good hull form, with vibration problems reduced by fitting a highly-skewed five- bladed propeller.

COASTWAY: innovative dredger

SHIPDUIIE!|L:... so. eee eee Merwede Shipyard (IHC Caland Group), The Netherlands
VeSSEl'S NAMEL  ccc  s as Coastway
CH TTT ic, erect etal cate «shave vspamsweniees 692
Owner/Operator...   Royal Boskalis
Westminster Dredging, The Netherlands
DESIQNES occ  s Merwede Shipyard, The Netherlands
Model tank establishment used  MARIN, The Netherlands
Total number of sister ships already completed)... 1
Total number of sister ships
SJ] @) 86) £0 [~) Son Nil

Dine ptimarily for mud and sand dredging, also shore replenishment work in shallow waters, including operations whilst beached (for which purpose extra hull strengthening has been built-in), Coastway and her sister Waterway incorporate many novel design features, as well as innovative energy-conservation concepts. The shallow-draught requirement, and the adoption of low L/B, and consequently, high B/T, ratios were examined during comprehensive model tests, aimed at developing a suitable hull form. Unusually for this type of vessel, a bulbous bow is fitted, and the tests also examined the effect of this on waterflow to the bow thruster, which, as a consequence, has been fitted in an angled position.

To avoid energy losses inherent in the conversion from mechanical to electrical power, and back to mechanical, the dredge and jet pumps ate driven directly from the forward ends of the main engines. The location of these pumps in the side buoyancy tanks amidships saves space; however, this has, together with the wide beam, necessitated the use of shaft lines from engine to propeller and pumps, respectively, which diverge at an angle of 7deg to the centteline. This arrangement was a major influence on the designers’ decision to adopt a 'twin-gondola' aft hull form.

Dredging is carried out over the starboard side, and uses a relatively short, 900mm diameter suction pipe (provision is made for extending this in the future, if required) and a single-walled dredge pump, driven through a variable- speed gearbox from the starboard main engine. The port main engine drives two jet pumps through a twin-speed gearbox, and these are available to fluidise and disperse spoil in the single, open hold, necessary when using the dredge pump to discharge over the bow, where a ball- jointed speed coupling is installed, or by 'rainbowing'. For these operations, the full power of the starboard engine is available to the pump, with the port unit utilised for manoeuvring and driving the shaft alternator. Alternatively, discharge can be accomplished through four hydraulically operated bottom drop doors, positioned on the centreline.
 22

The two main engines are of Wartsila 6L32 design, each developing 2760kW and drivirg a pair of Rolls-Royce
Kamewa CP propellers operating in nozzles, through propulsion gearboxes, with the port unit fitted with a PTO to a shaft alternator. The drive from the front end of the engines to the respective pumps is as described above.
Electrical power is also derived from an auxiliary diesel- driven alternator. Control of the dredging operation is maintained from an asymmetrical wheelhouse laid out for the three functions of dredge control, arranged to starboard; navigation, based on a central console; and catgo sutvey, carried out in a sepatate area to pott.

CONTSHIP AURORA: earning first
Germanischer Lloyd 'Green Passport

Shipbuilder:  Daewoo Shipbuilding & Marine

Engineering Co Ltd, Korea

VeSSEl'S NAMEL st s Contship Aurora

HUI] NUMDES occ ccc   cess ee een nens 4082

Owner/operator: 6 CP Ships/Contship

Containerlines, UK

Designer:  Daewoo Shipbuilding & Marine

Engineering Co Ltd, Korea
 (© eee UK
Total number of sister ships

HERE ate a number of ‘firsts’ which can be
 applied to the delivery of this vessel; these include being the first CP newbuilding to be deployed under the Contship Containerlines brand, and the first to operate on the line's re-vamped services from Europe to Austtalasia. However, of particular significance is the award to the vessel and its two sisters, of the first
GL 'Green Passport’ notation (this was made in
Hamburg after the delivery of the vessel, and is, therefore, not recorded in the ‘as-built’ class notation given below).

In essence, this document is a complete record of the environmental properties of the ship, and is available for port authorities to assess its compliance with international ecological requirements (which, in many cases, bring benefits such as reduced port fees). The re- organised trading pattern which Contship
Containerlines is opening up, in conjunction with its partners, replaces five current routes with two contta- rotating, fixed-day, weekly 'round the world 'schedules.
Operating eastbound via Suez and returning via Panama and east coast USA, and complemented by a westbound, reversed service, these will require 12 vessels, with Contship Aurora and her two sisters contributed by CP Ships.

The design provides for seven cargo holds forward of the machinery space, which is positioned 'semi-aft', and above which is an eight-tier accommodation block and navigating bridge. Behind this superstructure, space is arranged for loading 40ft and 45ft containers, part of a total of 2423TRU carried on deck. In the holds, cell guides secure 1698TEU, 739 of which are refrigerated.
In all, connections ate atranged for a massive number of 1304 reefer boxes, reflecting the current and future pattern of the northbound trade out of Australasia.

The 14 hatches are closed by a total of 41 liftaway panels designed by MacGregor to accept stack loadings of 90/120tonnes (TEU/FEU). Except for the bay adjacent to the engineroom, all holds can accept most dangerous cargoes classified by IMDG, and holds 2 to 7 have a mechanical supply and exhaust ventilation

SIGNIFICANT SHIPS OF 2002
 system providing 4500m’/h for each 40ft reefer container, and 3100m?/h/TEU.

The Sulzer JRTA96C-B main engine, which has been built in Korea by HSD Engine Co, develops 69,930bhp at 102rev/min and is directly coupled to an 8500mm
FP propeller, in an installation which registered maximum vibration levels of 4.4mm/sec on trials: well below a specified maximum of 7mm/sec. Two 3900kW, and two 3400kW, diesel-alternator sets satisfy electrical requirements, including supply to a 1600kW bow thruster.

CORAL PRINCESS: P&0O's first CODAG
 cruise liner

Shipbuilders... Chantiers de |'Atlantique, (Alstom), France

VESSEl'S NAME...  nees Coral Princess

HE inclusion of gas turbines in the propulsion
 systems of cruise liners has been noted in Significant
Ships previously (Millennium, presented in the 2000 edition, for example), where these aero-derivative machines have been combined with steam turbines in a
COGES (combined gas and steam turbine, integrated electric drive system) installation. Cora/ Princess now introduces another arrangement, CODAG (combined diesel engine and gas turbine); this brings together a
General Electric 25,000kW gas turbine driving an alternator, innovatively located in the funnel on deck 15, and two Wartsila/Alstom 16,200kW diesel- alternators, fitted in the main machinery room and using ptime movers of the maker's latest Enviroengine design, introduced to limit exhaust emissions and smoke. |

The three alternators supply power to two main switchboards and twin 20,000kW Alstom variable- speed propulsion motors. These drive FP propellers in an attangement which can provide 50% redundancy in case of fault, with back-up electrical supply available from two 1080kW diesel-alternator sets. The installation is served by a completely integrated automation system ptovided by Imtech, monitored in the engine control room, the safety centre, and wheelhouse. A somewhat similar CODAG arrangement has also been specified for P&O's two ships under construction at the
Nagasaki yard of Mitsubishi.

Coral Princess has accommodation for 2581 passengers and 960 crew, with the former using cabins on six decks (5, 8, 9, 10, 11 and 12), and public rooms spread over seven decks. All but 108 passenger cabins are positioned outboard, and a large number have balconies. Twenty cabins, including balconied and external types, are available for disabled travellers, and 400 ate equipped with ceiling-recessed sofa-beds for multiple occupancy.

An atrium served by two panoramic lifts, extends over four decks (5, 6, 7, and 8) and is the location of reception and tour offices. Two main restaurants ate
 28
 ptovided, and a 665-seat show lounge at the fore end of deck 6 is complemented by a two-tier multipurpose lounge at the aft end. Children and teenagers have space on deck 12, and the main pool and lido occupy deck 14, along with other outdoor amenities, some spreading over to deck 15. Twelve passenger lifts plus two adapted for wheelchair users, 11 service lifts, and four escalators linking the galleys and restaurants - all supplied by
MacGregor - are installed. Sewage treatment conforms to Alaskan regulations, and waste disposal meets the latest rules.

Lifesaving employs two complementary systems: two
RFD Marin-Ark 430-person MES installations using vertical chutes direct into enclosed liferafts, and 10
Fassmer lifeboats cartied under Navalimpianti stored- energy sliding-beam davits. Seven 109-person liferafts ate also provided. Also fitted in the hull are four Macor folding platforms for use when boarding tenders. Fire extinguishing systems feature a Marioff Hi-Fog water mist installation covering the entire vessel, including local points in the engineroom as requited by new IMO regulations, and low-location safety lighting is provided at escape routes and stairways.

 ferry for western Mediterranean service

Vessel’S NAME?    Danielle Casanova

NCREASING competition on the France/Corsica
 ferry links, demonstrated in SZgnificant Ships of 2007 with
Mega Express, is now further boosted by the introduction of Danielle Casanova by SNCM, designed to also operate on services between France, and Italy, Algeria and Tunisia, if required. A further ship of a different design, Pascal
Paol, was scheduled for completion early this year (2003) by Van der Giessen-de Noord.

The vessel has two vehicle decks available, with the fixed main deck (No 3) serving as the entry point, using twin tamp/doors at the stern, and a ramp/door and visor forward. A full range of MAFI-type trailers suitable for 20ft and 40ft containers, semi-trailers and lorries, can be stowed on 1000lane metres of deck, together with caravans and cars, with the latter also separately catered for on a hoistable deck (No 4) with a capacity for 350 cars. The forwatd and aft sections of this deck, port and starboard, can be raised or lowered to form ramps for access. When not in use, No 4 deck stows complete under the deckhead.

Ventilation of the gatage spaces gives 10 air changes hourly at sea, and 30 hourly when in port, based on empty space conditions. Side doors P&S at the aft end of No 3 deck allow access for provision containers, which are passed by lift to the storerooms on deck 2.

Decks 5, 6,7, 8 and 9 provide accommodation for a total of 2453 passengers in 10 two-plus-two deluxe suites: 374 two-plus-two cabins with private toilets, and 82 without: also four two-plus-two cabins for disabled travellers.
Pullman seats are arranged for 320 passengers, and 200 mote are allocated space on deck.

Public spaces on decks 9 and 10 provide restaurants, lounges, shops, conference facilities and disco, with playroom, bats, swimming pool, and sun deck also available. Four 20-person elevators allow movement between decks. Crew accommodation serves 174 personnel, with senior officers occupying eight apartments and others allocated 160 single-berth, and three twin-berth cabins, all with private facilities. Lifesaving equipment includes four MES installations and 6 x 150-person lifeboats. Anti-heeling and roll stabilisation machinery is fitted to improve passenger comfort.

The propulsion plant comprises four Wartsila 9L46C main engines, each developing 9450kW MCR at
 30
 500rev/min. Each pair is connected through a twin- input/single-output Maag reduction gearbox to a CP propeller running at 154rev/min. Electrical power is generated by five Wartsila/A van Kaick 2950kVA diesel- alternator sets. Two Lips 1600kW bow thrusters are fitted and the twin rudders are of the flap type.

EURO SQUALL:
 class container ship

Shipbuilder:  Peters Schiffoau AG, Germany
Vessel'S NAMEL sss s Euro Squall
HUI] NUMDES  eee   eee e eee  t nees 672
Owner/operatOr: s M/S ‘Euro Squall’
GmbH & Co KG, Germany
Designer:  Peters Schiffoau AG, Germany a | UK
Total number of sister ships already COMpleted) teet s 1
Total number of sister ships
SUI CPT BAST. meee ees nme dE KaSIRG FFI EsoINE HK Nil

DeeaANS a profile typical of that developed over tecent years by European builders and operators of this class of vessel, Euro Squall is single- decked, double-hulled, and has three holds. The fore patt of No 1 cargo hold is accessed through an extended, stepped forecastle, by way of No 1 hatch (12.30m long x 13.24/7.94m wide). The other hatches work through the upper deck with No 2 (12.30m x 16.00m) serving the aft portion of No 1 hold and Nos 3 and 4 (both 25.20m x 16.00m) covering Nos 2 and 3 holds.

The six-tier, plus wheelhouse, accommodation block, is positioned right aft, leaving clear deck space above the engineroom and aft of the hatches, to stow two
TEU stacks. In all, 681 TEU can be loaded: 493 on deck and hatch covers, and 188 in the holds. The hatch covers ate steel folding type, operated by external hydraulic cylinders and are complete with container fittings and lashing points. No 1 hold is fitted for the cattiage of dangerous goods, and 110 reefer plugs are provided. As a general cargo carrier, Euro Squall has a
 grain capacity of 10,300m’ and a bale measurement of
 9975m’. No cargo geat is fitted.

The double-hull structure incorporates a 'tween deck' at the top, P&S, serving as fore and aft passageways, and has the side spaces arranged as water ballast tanks, separated from similar tanks in the double bottom. Fuel capacity is provided in four, athwartship deep tanks, fitted with thermal-oil heating, and built between Nos 2 and 3 holds, whilst wing tanks at the aft end of No 3 hold form gas oil and slop tanks.

The main engine is a Mak 8M43 type, running on heavy fuel, and having an MCR of 7200kW at 500rev/min. A Vulkan Rato flexible coupling connects with a Renk gearbox which reduces the speed to

SIGNIFICANT SHIPS OF 2002
 176rev/min to drive the Lips CP propeller, and also has a power take-off to an A van Kaick alternator. Two A van Kaick diesel-alternator-sets using Volvo prime movets ate also available. Manoeuvrability is assisted by a Jastram bow thruster, which provides a thrust of 5.5tonnes, and by an Ulstein flap-type rudder.

EURO SUN: STX keeps open the
Daedong tanker portfolio

Shipbuilder:  STX Shipbuilding Co Ltd, Korea
VESSEI'S NAME...  ssssss rees Euro Sun
HUI] NUMDES eee  sssss s ss S-1091
Owner/operator  Euroceanica CTGM SA, Switzerland
Designer  STX Shipbuilding Co Ltd, Korea
Model test establishment used:.. KRISO, Korea
EI ota elie <a ptbeng peed actedt egSeca pues Marshall Islands
Total number of sister ships already completed: Nil (2 for other owners)

Still ON OPPs...   r  3 (plus others for different owners)

ESPITE the financial problems of recent years, 
Daedong Shipbuilding continued to attract orders, mainly for vessels selected from the company's extensive portfolio of product tanker designs, and particularly the 45,800dwt/46,000dwt
MR tange. Work on these designs has continued following the reconstitution of the company under the name STX Shipbuilding within the Ssangyong group (well known for diesel engine construction), and Euro Sun is the first of four vessels delivered by the new company to Swiss operator Euroceanica.
Two similar hulls have been built for another owner, and 28 further deliveries are understood to be programmed for further European operators.

Suitable for worldwide trading, the design is single- decked with forecastle, and features a double hull containing a cargo space divided into six pairs of tanks by corrugated, longitudinal, and transverse bulkheads. Side stiffening is contained within the double hull, and the structure of the cambered upper deck is external, thus presenting a flush surface in the tanks. A high percentage of mild steel - 75% of the total steelweight - is used.

Euro Sun is designed to carry crude and product oils, also IMO Type III chemicals. Some 120 commodities, including caustic soda, can be loaded in tanks which are coated with pure epoxy. The piping system is designed for six cargo segregations, with each tank fitted with a Frank Mohn (Framo) hydraulic, submerged pump (total 12) rated at 600m*/h. Two slop tanks are provided and served by two 300m?/h pumps. Water ballast capacity is 24,390m’, carried in tanks coated with a light coloured, tar-free epoxy, and handled by two 750m°’/h pumps.

Hyundai Heavy Industries built the MAN B&W 6S5OMC (Mk 6) main engine which develops 11,640bhp full power at 127rev/min. Three sets of
Hyundai 740kW alternators powered by Ssangyong- built MAN B&W diesel engines supply the electrical power, with steam produced in an 18,000kg/h
Aalborg oil-fired boiler, and a 1200kg/h/1150kg/h composite boiler. Accommodation is provided for a complement of 15 officers and 12 crew, plus six
Suez/repait crew, in the deckhouse aft.

 ‘RNANDO TAPIAS has been built for the transportation of liquefied natural gas (LNG), and special attention has been paid in her design to the facilities at loading and discharging berths worldwide, to ensure compatibility of mooring fender contact areas, gangway and manifold positions, and other aspects. Laid out with a continuous single deck, and a sunken aft deck, the vessel has four cargo tanks, constructed on the Gaz
Transport membrane system (GT No 96 E[-2), positioned in a double-hull configuration which is extended above the deck to form a trunk.
Gas is maintained at a temperature of -163°C with a maximum boil-off rate of less than 0.15% fully
 dfrminpgements, loaded cargo volume. Primary/secondary barriers of
 36% nickel-steel (Invar) alloy are fitted in the cargo tanks, with plywood boxes filled with expanded pearlite insulation, Underdeck passageways in the trunk deck space provide fore and aft access, and a duct keel in the double bottom serves as a pipe and cable passage.
The cargo handling systems are designed to load/ discharge cargo within 12hours, using two sets of 1700m°/h pumps and one stripping/ spray pump in each tank, forming a complete assembly within a ‘tripod mast’ construction with Saab radar beam-type
 forming primary/secondary
 level gauges, and all fittings and pipes. Vapour handling equipment such as two high-duty
 compressors, two low-duty compressors, one main and one forcing vaporiser, and two boil-off/warm- up heaters, are arranged in the cargo machinery room, 
The main propulsion unit is a Kawasaki UA-360 steam turbine, developing 36,000shp at 88rev/min (behind the gearbox), The unit has high- and low-pressure turbines, double-
 cross-compound

SIGNIFICANT SHIPS OF 2002
 reduction gearing, main condenser, and astern turbine. Two dual-fuel boilers supply steam for the main turbine, steam auxiliaries, and turbo-generators.
Two of the latter are installed for electrical power together with one diesel-powered set. Each develops a similar output of 3450kW. Current is transmitted to three switchboards (main, cargo, and emergency) located in separate rooms, An integrated automation system is fitted, and control and monitoring is carried out from the cargo and engine control rooms.

Accommodation and navigation spaces are arranged right aft, with the latter designed for
Optimum operational safety around a modular workstation, fitted for one-man operation, and meeting all design and layout requirements of the BV notation +SYS-NEQ-1

FINNFIGHTER: versatile multi-access ro-lo forest products carrier

Shipbuilder:  eee Stocznia Gdynia

SA, Poland
Vessel'S NAME  0...  s  Finntighter
Hull number:  Lesesveses, 8222/01

Owner/operator:  B&N Nordsjéfrakt, 
Sweden
Designer  Stocznia Gdynia SA, Poland
Model test establishment used  CTO, Poland
 sc @ Sweden
Total number of sister ships
 already completed: :   eee neces Nil
Total number of sister ships

Still Ty GAGE... nce an niGinnrsieas@sumrmncwramrosesoue 2

Note: Illustration shows sister ship Finnwood.

E demands of Baltic forest products trades have
 brought about the introduction of a succession of
 specialist ship designs over recent years, all primarily aimed at achieving high-quality handling, and safe transportation of timber, paper, and pulp cargoes. In this latest and most interesting series, three vessels (plus one option) built at

Gdynia, not only incorporate a comprehensive outfit of

MacGregor rolling, and lift-on/lift off, access systems to ensure speedy cargo handling, but they meet the requirements of a number of backhaul cargoes. The latter include china clay from Savannah to Europe, which can be inttoduced, as required, into a loading schedule which would normally comprtise paper to the USA from Finland ot Zeebrugge, and a return with newsprint from Canada to the UK and the Continent.

The double-skin hull is marked by a whaleback protection over the forecastle, and by the structure which encloses the side loading system, built into the starboard side of No 3 hold. This contains three independent cargo elevators, each of 16tonnes capacity, serving tweendeck and tank top levels. Rolling access to the vessel is by means of a stern ramp/door, with forward movement into the tweendecks, which provides 780 lane metres stowage for trailers, obtained through No 4 hold.

Internal movement is controlled by watertight doors (one top-hinged and one side-hinged) at each end of this level.

Folding hatch cover sets ate fitted to weatherdeck levels of
 holds 1, 2, and 4, and also to the tweendeck level of No 2, with the former designed to accept container stack loads of
 56tonnes (TEU) and 65tonnes (FEU), and the latter atranged for a uniform loading of 8tonnes/m’. By varying the combination of opening/closing the bulkhead doors, and the folding cover in No 2 tweendeck (which is non-WT in the closed position and dust-tight when open, using the
Hydro-Seal system), between three and five cargo separations can be carried at one time. Thete are, in addition, two side-hinged bulkhead doots on the tanktop, between holds 1 and 2, and 2 and 3. A comprehensive outfit of cargo securing fittings also form part of this complex MacGregor installation.

The main superstructure is arranged right aft, with the engineroom containing two Wartsila 6L46 main engines, positioned forward of this under No 4 hold. This installation produces 2 x 6300kW at 550rev/min and drives a CP propeller through a Renk twin-input/single-output gearbox. A 1750kVA alternator is powered from the gearbox, with additional electrical supply derived from

SIGNIFICANT SHIPS OF 2002
 three 1285kVA diesel-alternator sets. A Becker high-lift rudder is fitted aft and a 1200kW thruster operates forward.

HE 'Hurtigrute’ or "Norwegian Coastal Voyage’ was
 inaugurated in 1893 and runs along the Norwegian west and north coast from Bergen to Kirkenes. Over the yeats it has developed into a world-renowned toutist attraction, with the two current partners in the operation, OVDS and Troms Fylkes Dampskibsselskap (TFDS), regularly and simultaneously adding new tonnage, as ptesented in several editions of Significant Ships (most recently, Nordkapp and Polarlys, in 1996). Although both owners offer generally similar facilities on theit respective vessels, it is notable that each retains a completely independent approach to machinery specification, and often introduces innovative technical features into their enginerooms, as is the case with three new vessels of the 'Millennium' class.

Details of the choices made by TFDS ate given in the later report on Tro//ford. However, for consort and subject vessel Finnmarken, OVDS has selected a twin- screw, 'father-and-son' machinery installation, with each transmission line utilising a 9L32 and a 6L32 Wartsila diesel engine, arranged forward and aft, respectively, of a twin-input/ single-output reduction gearbox, with the six- cylinder units aligned inboard of the shafts and the larger engines.

Each gearbox drives a vatiable-frequency 1730kKVA/2300kVA Concycle alternator/motor, also positioned inboard of the nine-cylinder engines, whilst the smaller engines can connect with the main gearing or, from their free ends, with two 2500kVA alternator sets.
This complex arrangement is designed to give maximum engine loading and fuel economy, and allows five operating modes to be selected, depending on which combination of main engine and alternator/motor is used.
 46

A special feature of the installation is the first use of
Wartsila efficiency rudders (formerly known as
PropacRudders), which have a fixed, torpedo-shaped bulb built on to each flap-rudder, and faired into the highly skewed CP propellers. With two Brunvoll 'super-silent'- type tunnel thrusters forward and an Ulstein retractable (swing-up) azimuth thruster aft, Fimumarken is well equipped to manoeuvte easily in the narrowest channels and under difficult conditions.

Fulfilling its prime function as a supply vessel for the local communities, Finnmarken has three refrigerated cargo holds, dry cargo space, and accommodation for around 55 cats on deck 2. Access for all cargo is through a
MacGregor side-port on deck 3, with a lift linking cargo decks for internal distribution. On international cruises, 643 passengers can be carried, increased to 1000 on the
Hurtigrute service and berthed in 285 cabins, 87% of which are outboard. These, and the public spaces, have been designed with early 1900s décor in mind, with the latter offering travellers as full a range of facilities as now presented as standard on cruising vessels, including, for the first time on the Norwegian Coastal Service since the first Finnmarken in 1912, an outdoor swimming pool.

FS VANESSA: Portuguese-built 15,500dwt products/chemical tanker for French owner

Shipbuilder:  Estaleiros Navais de Viana
 do Castelo SA (ENVC), Portugal

VESSEI'S NAMEL  eee  ssseees n FS Vanessa

VANESSA is constructed with a double hull forming common side and bottom water ballast tanks, and has a cargo space divided into 10 cargo tanks by a centreline, and six transverse bulkheads. All structute, piping, and pump internals in contact with catgo, ate constructed of grade 316L stainless steel, and with stiffening arranged outside the cargo spaces, a plain internal surface is presented, to which a 3 x 100microns phenolic epoxy coating has been applied.

A pait of deep tanks forward ate designated for heavy fuel, and a ttansverse pumptoom separates the engineroom and the cargo space. The design is arranged for the carriage of IMO II cargoes with a specific gravity of 1.025tonnes/m*’ for filling up to 98% full, and for a partial loading of products with a maximum density of 1.54tonnes/m*. In addition, two cylindrical slop tanks are mounted on deck.

Cargo is handled by 10 Framo 300m*/h hydraulically operated submerged pumps serving the main tanks, and two similar 100m’/h units working the slop tanks.
Loading and discharge is accomplished through a deck manifold at midships P&S. Tanks ate heated by a thermal system capable of maintaining cargo temperatures at 70°C. All cargo and slop tank valves ate fitted with a pressute/vacuum valve and a flame- arresting device, and a vapour return collector for shore return of gas is provided. For cleaning purposes, each tank is fitted with two fixed appliances, capable of using either hot or cold seawater, or fresh water.

FS Vanessa is fitted with a MaK 7M43 main engine developing 6300kW MCR when running at 500rev/min. Drive to the CP propeller is by way of a
Volda reduction gearbox having an output speed of 150rev/min, and this has a 1500kW PTO connection to an AEM shaft generator to satisfy electrical requirements at sea. A power take-in arrangement allows the shaft alternator to transmit a similar amount of power from the auxiliary alternators in an emergency; this gives the ship a minimum speed in a loaded condition of 7knots. Three 750kW diesel- alternator sets are also installed in an engineroom, where machinery can be operated locally, from the engine control room, or bridge and wings.

GRAND PIONEER: Japanese-built car carrier

S181] ©) 010] | (6 (<n Shin Kurushima

HE demand for cars and other road vehicles to
 be carried around the world continues unabated, and with it comes a requirement for ships to do the transporting, From years of experience, owners and builders have, more or less, developed a standard layout for the juggernauts of this trade, and it is only in telatively minor details (excluding choice of machinery, subcontractors, and similar features) that individual vessels of this class differ.

For example, a quick comparison between Grand
Pioneer and Crystal Ray, the last large car-carrier to feature in Significant Ships (2000), shows that the first- named has side-entrances port and starboard, whilst the latter manages with only one - positioned just forwatd of the engineroom, whereas Grand Pioneer favours a midships access. Both ships do, however, have quarter ramps, aft on the starboard side. Other than that, perhaps the only external difference is in the layout of the accommodation block.

Internally, Grand Pioneer has 12 car decks, two of which are hoistable, plus a garage deck, covering three-quarters of the hull on the upper deck, behind the deckhouse. Access, as already noted, is over stern and side ramp/doors, with the former carrying a designed load of 150tonnes. Movement between decks is by a series of fixed ramps. The vessel's design has been produced with particular emphasis on the carriage of large-sized vehicles, notably buses, dump-trucks and bulldozers, and on maximising the stowage of passenger cars, with a capacity of 6501 claimed.

The main engine is a Mitsubishi 8UECO6OLS unit built by Kobe Diesel, and developing 14,160kW at
 50
 100trev/min. A FP propeller, fitted with a Turbo
Ring, drives the vessel at a speed of 20knots, with the engine running at 85% full power. Electrical supply comes from three 960kW diesel-alternator sets, and a 1155kW bow thruster is fitted.

GRETA-C: successful owner/builder
 combination

ShiPDUIIer:... eee Damen Shipyards, Hoogezand, The Netherlands/ Damen Shipyards, Galati, Romania

VESSEl'S NAME!    s aeeaes Greta-C

Hull NUMDES ccc ccc   eee ae anaees 823

Owner/operator  Carisbrooke Shipping Ltd, 
United Kingdom

DESIGNED  s Damen Shipyards, 
The Netherlands

Model test establishment used  MARIN, 
The Netherlands

FLAG.  seaeeaeneeneneeens UK
Total number of sister ships
 already COMPIeted: : seettt r s Nil
Total number of sister ships

PROSPERING association between owner and
 builder over the past 20 years has resulted from these two companies recognising the need for change and diversification. For Carisbrooke, this has meant widening its interests to become involved in worldwide trading with larger ships, whilst for Damen, bringing together the economies of Eastern European and Far
Eastern shipbuilding costs, and the technical skills inherent in the builder's Western base, has been the key to its expansion. Grefa-C demonstrates the effects of these strategies: entering service with a deadweight capacity neatly ten times greater than was the fleet average 20 yeats ago.

Basing much of its output on standard 'Combi-
Freighter’ hulls has helped the shipyard's performance, and Grefa-C introduces the '19000G' variant of that range - the largest to date. The design features a double- skin hull with side and bottom spaces allocated for ballast and bunker purposes, and these surround two box-shaped holds 37.68m and 72.84m long and 20.20m wide. Depth under the covers is 12.28m, and the spaces ate suitable for containerised or break-bulk cargoes, with mechanical ventilation at 12 air changes/hour and dehumidification available.

The tank top is strengthened for a maximum uniform loading of 15tonnes/m/, and container stack loading is 100tonnes/TEU or 130tonnes/FEU. MacGregor high- stowing covers serve Nos 1 and 2 hatches with two lift- away panels fitted between them to complete the closure. A uniformly distributed weather load of 1.75tonnes/m? and container stack loadings of 50tonnes (TEU) and 65tonnes (FEU) can be sustained.
The tweendeck openings are closed by non- weathertight pontoon covers, which can also be used to form grain divisions, and deck cargo is protected by a breakwater at the forecastle.

Cargo handling is by three slim-line Liebherr cranes built on pedestals into the starboard side structure, with each capable of lifting 30tonnes/40tonnes/60tonnes at 32m/30m/18m outreach. As well as cargo duties, the cranes are used for hatch cover and grain division handling, 
The main engine is a MAN-B&W 6S46MC-C type rated at 7860kW at 129rev/min. and connected by a
Vulkan flexible rubber coupling to a tunnel gearbox.
Vulkan also supplied the multi-disc coupling which links

SIGNIFICANT SHIPS OF 2002
 the gearbox power take-off with a 1500kW Leroy
Somer LSA 52M7 alternator. Two 781kVA diesel- alternator sets are also provided. Manoeuvrability at dead-slow speeds is enhanced by a 750kW electric bow thruster with CP propeller, fitted in a transverse tunnel.

 "ARAD is the first of four VLCCs ordered
 originally by Greek owner Hellespont, but sold on to NSCSA during construction, and, as such, the design retains much that was specified in the initial contract. These features are particularly concerned with the strength and future structural life of the vessel, and include adopting a beam of 58m in order to reduce hull deflection, and strengthening the ptimary members by adding double structures and buttresses in order to reduce hull deformation by 10%.

The requirements of ABS Safehull and LR
ShipRight have been incorporated, and an FEA of the hull structures in different loading conditions has been cartied out, in line with the vessel's designed fatigue life, in excess of 40 years. The hull weight includes 45% high-tensile steel, with 'Z' quality used for the outermost strakes of the double bottom, and 'E' grade in the lowest hopper strake. Structural design has reduced the number of individual components used in construction, thereby benefiting future maintenance.

Fifteen cargo tanks are arranged within a double hull designated for segregated water ballast, and there ate also two slop tanks. The pumping system allows a maximum discharge rate of 15,000m°/h using three
 56
 5000m°’/h steam turbine-driven pumps, which can handle three grades of oil simultaneously through a two-valve segregation arrangement. Cargo loading rate is 20,000m°/h. Radar-type tank level gauges are fitted, and a double-scrubber inert gas system reduces SO: levels to less than 2ppm. This system also gives full coverage to the ballast tanks, cutting cotrosion levels of steelwork and _ piping considerably. Gas level monitoring is applied to all tanks, ballast spaces and pumprooms, and displayed in the cargo control room.

The main engine has been constructed by Samsung (HSD) to a Sulzer 8RTA84T-D design, developing an
MCR of 32,825kW at 76rev/min. The service speed is 16.10knots at an output of 26,780kW, including a 15% sea margin. Electrical requirements are catered for by three Wartsila/ Hyundai diesel-alternator sets, each producing 1200kW at 720rev/min, and two boilers each supply 45tonnes/h steam to the cargo systems. The vessel is also fitted with an integrated navigation system, which supports the operation of vatious bridge activities, including route planning and manoeuvring.

HELLESPONT ALHAMBRA:
 re-introducing the ULCC - with extra quality

Shipbuilder:   Daewoo Shipbuilding & Marine

Engineering Co Ltd, Korea

Vessel'’S Nam  Hellespont Alhambra
 010101001 0\~] Ger 5183

Owner/operator:  Hellespont Steamship

Corp, Gr 

Designer  Daewoo Shipbuilding & Marine

Engineering Co Ltd, Korea

Model test establishment USEd)  cee -
 a6 re Marshall Islands
Total number of sister ships already
 (ole) a0) ©) (=) (-\0 nce Nil

Total number of sister ships still on order:  3

WENTY-FIVE years after the last ULCC entered
 service, the concept has been revived with this four-ship class incorporating, not only the now mandatory double-hull (the vessels are the largest tankers of this type ever built), but a whole range of innovative features, mostly developed by the owner's technical department. First of series Hellespont
Alhambra has a single continuous deck, sunk at the aft end, with a double hull containing five pairs of combined side and bottom water ballast tanks.
Within this structure, two longitudinal bulkheads form port, starboard and centre cargo spaces, divided by transverse divisions into seven wing tanks each side (plus two slop tanks), and five centre tanks. All ate arranged to carry crude oil having a flash point below 60°C, handled by three 5000m°/h steam turbine pumps in a three-segregation cargo system.

Central to the owner's design philosophy is a requirement for a minimum 40-year ship-life without major steel replacement, emphasised by including above-rule scantlings, and by extending the 'double- scrubbing’ inert-gas system, successfully fitted to the cargo tanks of existing crude carriers, to also setve the ballast spaces. The system avoids corrosion of steel tank internals by substantially reducing the relatively high percentage of sulphur dioxide (SO2) present in inert gases, which, under certain circumstances, can combine with water to form highly corrosive sulphuric acid.

Use of this system has been enhanced by the development, by Hellespont technical personnel, of a software programme, VENT2D, which can be used to model air and gas flows through the complex structure of double-hull spaces. It offers a solution to the problems of delivering air to points furthest away from the gas inlets.

A novel choice of a white colour scheme applied above the waterline, is aimed at reflecting radiant energy and reducing steelwork temperatures, thereby

SIGNIFICANT SHIPS OF 2002
 safeguarding the condition of coatings at the tops of tanks, and generally benefiting the steel structure. A rigorous approach to steel preparation and coating has been adopted, especially in ballast spaces, where two coats of metal-filled, pure epoxy have been applied to re-blasted steel finished to a 'smooth-to- touch! condition, and a five-year zinc anode cathodic protection system is fitted.

Unusually, navigation bridge wings extending to the ship's side have been eliminated to reduce vibration and windage, giving a small power saving, but with a consequent loss of view to the navigators. The latter is compensated for by fitting closed-circuit television cameras. However, Hellespont expects that much loading and discharge will be at offshore buoys so wings will be of minimal use. Another owner's choice is to have the wheelhouse erected on pillars, three decks above the main superstructure.

Unlike the 1970s group of ULCCs, which were steam turbine driven, Hellespont Alhambra is fitted with a nine-cylinder Sulzer RTA84T-D main engine developing 36,911kW at 76rev/min. At 95% MCR, this gives a loaded service speed of 16.00knots, and a ballast speed with 15% sea margin of almost 18.00knots.

HONG KONG EXPRESS:
 another ‘biggest'
 from Hyundai's box-ship portfolio

Shipbuilder: 0.0... Hyundai Heavy Industries

Co Ltd, Korea

Vessel's NaMme?... eee Hong Kong Express

Aull MUMDE| Foo... ccc  s sseeess saees 1365

Owner/operator:  ec  s s Hapag-Lloyd

Container Line, Germany

DOSIQNET! oo...  s Hyundai Heavy

Industries Co Ltd, Korea

FLAG! eee  sssss rsteeesettreeessnees Germany
Total number of sister ships
 already Completed: ccc  ss ts s 2
Total number of sister ships
 <1] Ke) ake] 6 (=) seen 1
 [* Significant Ships of 2001 we tecorded the completion of the 'biggest yet’ box ship (Hyundai's
P&O Nedlloyd Stuyvesant - 6802TEU), with the qualification that the shipyard was already planning vessels with 10% greater TEU intake. That larger seties is now a reality, with four vessels scheduled to enter setvice during an eight-month period from October 2002.

They are capable of lifting 7500TEU, with the extra capacity gained, in the main, by increasing length bp by 18m, which allows 3742TEU to be carried in the holds and 3758 on deck. The growing market in the long-haul tefrigerated trades is served by making 700 reefer connections available. The arrangement provides for 20ft, 40ft, and 45ft containers to be cartied, with cell guides for 20ft and 40ft units fitted in the holds, and athwartship lashing bridges from which 6/7 tiers can be secured, on the upper deck.

The structure has been designed to Hyundai's gitderless system. This provides an ‘open-hatch' configuration, closed by MacGregor lift-away pontoon type hatch covers fitted with Lubripad bearing pads. In all, 55 panels are used, arranged in single-, double-, and triple-panel combinations to serve the eight holds, positioned seven forward of the engineroom, and one aft. Container stack loadings of up to 120tonnes are achievable on the covers, depending on position and size of box. Two air changes hourly are provided for Nos 2/4 holds, where dangerous cargoes of SOLAS classes 2 to 8 can be carried.

Hong Kong Express operates at a service speed of 25.30knots with the main engine running at 90% MCR and no power absotbed by the shaft alternator, but allowing 20% sea margin. Electrical requirements are served from a large 4500kW alternator driven by power take-off from the main engine, and by four sets with separate diesel prime-movers, two of which ate rated at
 64
 4000kW, and one each at 2300kW and 1750kW.
Performance of the vessel is enhanced by the application to the outside shell of a tin-free, self- polishing antifouling paint with a five-year life, and the installation of an impressed-current cathodic protection system.

SIGNIFICANT SHIPS OF 2002
  military sealift duties

Shipbuilder:  Flensburger Schiffbau-

Gesellschaft (FSG), Germany

VESSEI'S NAMEL s t s Hurst Point
 (011600) 0\=) Oe 717

Owner/operator:  AWSR Shipping / 
Andrew Weir Shipping Ltd, UK

DOSIQNES oo...    ss sss s FSG, Germany/ 
Houlder Offshore Engineering, UK

Model test establishment used  HSVA, 
Germany

FLAG!  s ss sestessesa s ns UK
Total number of sister
 ships already completed... Nil
Total number of sister
 ships still on order:  3 at FSG, 2 at

Harland & Wolff, UK

UILT to conform to a new UK Ministry of

Defence initiative aimed at satisfying its sealift requirements, Hurst Point is the first of six vessels that will be used for routine military operations for parts of each yeat, and released for normal commercial traffic at other times. Under the terms of a 20-year charter, nominal owner AWSR Shipping is also required to have the vessels available at short notice for MOD use, in times of emergency.

The design and hull form is based upon that of a successful series developed by FSG for Turkish owner
UND (UND Akdeniz; Significant Ships of 2000), but particularly modified by extending the double-hull structure to upper deck level to improve damage stability. In addition, the main deck can withstand 500mm of free water, in line with the Stockholm
Agreement, and cross-flooding arrangements are aimed at ensuring that an upright position can be maintained even under extensive damage conditions.

Vehicles are carried on three levels, with entry at the main deck over a 150tonne capacity, 17m wide x 13.7m long stern ramp, adapted to work with Mexefloat pontoons, supplemented by a 75tonne, starboard side ramp, forwatd of midships, which can operate within + 20deg limits. Internal movement to the upper deck is by a fixed ramp closed by a guillotine-type door, whilst a similar ramp to the lower hold has a hinged cover.

There are 2620lane metres available on decks which ate suitable for 5tonnes/m? loading, whilst a clear height of 6.8m allows the main deck space to accommodate helicopters, and two tiers of 9ft 6in high containers on trailers. Container capacity is 656TEU, loaded by a starboard side, 40tonne swl bulwark-mounted crane.
Upper deck stowage is protected by a forecastle whaleback, which also contains two Flume passive stabilisation tanks.

Two seven-cylinder Caterpillar Mak M43 main engines are fitted (two vessels of the class, expected to work primarily in the commercial mode, will use the nine-cylinder variant specified for UND Akdeniz).
Developing 6300kW each, these drive inward-turning
CP propellers to give a service speed of 18.00knots when running at 79% MCR with a 22% sea margin.
Excellent manoeuvrability is achieved from a 180kN
 66
 bow thruster, and two independently operable, high-lift rudders, which include Costa bulbs.

The main superstructure, fitted forward of the machinery space, incorporates a wheelhouse with totally enclosed bridge wings (different from the Turkish seties), each containing a full set of controls, and which complies with the PSNR class notation, indicating fully redundant propulsion and steering. Accommodation is provided for a complement of 18 plus 12 drivers.

KAISHUU: marking IZAR's move into the dredger market

Shipbuilder  IZAR (Astillero Sestao), Spain
VESSEl'S NAME!  teneees Kaishuu
HUI] NUMDES:  ccc   cee  n s 320
Owner/operator:... TOA (LUX) SA, Luxembourg
Designer: 0.. IZAR (Astillero Sestao), Spain
FAG): nc ssresncerawersomever reper scnnnn sittin 1 Aas Luxembourg
Total number of sister ships
 already COMpIeted)    teens Nil
Total number of sister ships

Still ON OFCOM...   teeteet s 1

N a move to break into a new niche market, the

IZAR Group has been successful in winning contracts to build two dredgers for companies in the
Belgian Jan de Nul Group (plus two smaller units for French owners, to be built at the Juliana yard).
Kaishuu and a sister, with a capacity of 16,500m’, are some 5000m° larger than their consorts, and will be employed by Singaporean associates of de Nul in land reclamation and civil engineering work in
South-East Asia.

The design has main machinery aft, and wheelhouse and accommodation forward, with the hopper, contained within wide side spaces, arranged with sloping bottom and seven drop-doors, positioned between. Dredging uses port and starboard suction pipes positioned aft of midships and dimensioned to dredge at a depth of 34m below the keel, at an angle of 50deg. Three davits with hydraulic winches handle the suction pipes and the draghead, which is fitted with an adjustable visor and wave compensator. Spoil is discharged through the drop doots, or over the bow, either through a floating hose, or by rainbow nozzle.

The machinery installation is centred upon two
MAN B&W 8L48/60 main engines, each connected aft by a shaftline, a flexible coupling and a reduction geatbox, to a CP propeller. A power take-off from each of these gearboxes provides the drive, by way of multiplier gearboxes and flexible couplings, to the port and starboard dredge pumps. These are situated in the aft pump room and ate rated at 25,200m’/h and 21,600m°/h, respectively. Two more gearbox power take-offs drive the two 4000kW alternators which satisfy the vessel's electrical requirements. The pumps ate supplied by IHC, and have double walls and an abrasive-resistant internal coating. To speed discharge, high-pressure water can be directed into the hopper through nozzles to assist fluidisation, and a de-gassing plant is fitted to the suction pipe in the aft pumproom.

An Imtech automation system, which integrates the navigation DP/DT systems, alarm monitoring and dredging control, is operated from a large triangular-shaped wheelhouse and navigating bridge, designed to afford all-round visability for both navigating and dredging personnel. Manoeuvring and positioning are enhanced by the fitting of two bow thrusters, and one stern unit.

LA SUPERBA: luxury cruise-ferry for
Grimaldi services

Shipbuilders...    s Nuovi Cantieri
Apuania Spa (NCA), Italy
VeSSEI'S NAME Le  s La Superba
Hull NMUMDES eee c     esses s aeaees C 1220
Owner/operator:  Grandi Navi Veloci (GNV)/Grimaldi Lines, Italy
D@SIQNEDS: s s s Nuovi Cantieri
Apuania SpA/Studio De Jorio, Italy lo \0 ee Italy
Total number of sister ships already Completed)...   Nil
Total number of sister ships <i] | RO) ©) 60 (=) 1

HE varied activities of Grimaldi Lines are based
 on deep-sea services covering the Mediterranean and Northern Europe, West Africa, and South
America, utilising an expanding fleet of multipurpose ro-ro freight carriers. Alongside these operations, the Group is also heavily involved with satisfying the demand for regular ro-pax shipping links between north and south Italy, aimed at easing heavy congestion on the country's motorways. Ferry services are also operated between the mainland and
Sardinia. It is for these two sectors of its business that Grimaldi has built the very fast La Superba and
La Suprema - two vessels which maintain the luxury cruise element introduced on to these routes with
Majestic, delivered from the same builder in 1993, and featured in Significant Ships of that year.

The propulsion plant is understood to be one of the most powerful yet fitted to a ship of this type, and comprises four Wartsila 16V46C main engines, each developing 16,800kW and driving twin screws through a pair of  twin-input/single-output gearboxes. Particular attention has been paid by Renk in the design of these units to ensure low levels of noise and vibration, notably by using double-helical teeth with special geometry, and arranging additional stiffening in the gear casing walls. Four alternators powered by Wartsila 6R32LNE 2360kW prime movers satisfy the electrical requirements.

Vehicles are carried on four fixed and one hoistable deck, providing 3230 lane metres of space for 719 cars, accessed through a stern door/ramp, with
 72
 internal transfer by fixed and moveable ramps.
Passenger accommodation is arranged for 3000 persons in a layout developed by leading Italian designer Studio De Jorio. This features two restaurants and cafés, a swimming pool spa, gym and hairdresser, bars, a créche, games room, discotheque, casino, cinema, and shops. Passengers desiring quieter pastimes can use a card room, library, and chapel, whilst conference requirements are also provided for.

A total of 567 cabins, comprising six grand suites, six other suites, 526 standard cabins, and four berths for handicapped travellers, are supplemented by pullman seating for 940 passengers. Safety requirements are satisfied by the statutory complement of lifeboats and rafts, enhanced by four 450-person MES installations.

LNG RIVERS: more variety from Hyundai

Shipbuilders... eee  s Hyundai Heavy

Industries Co Ltd, Korea

VeSSEl'S NAMEL  ccc  tettt s LNG Rivers

HUI] NUMDES:... eee e   ete  n nennees 1295

Owner/operator  Bonny Gas Transport Ltd, 
Nigeria

Designer:  Hyundai Heavy Industries Co Ltd, 
Korea

FIAC): aetrmncermonserrense:rarssneees penne commen ad aiid nA Bermuda
Total number of sister ships
 already COMpIeted  cc  s Nil
Total number of sister ships

Still ON OFOP oo...   ee ttttn nes 2

NG Rivers is the first of three vessels ordered from
 yundai, to satisfy her owner's 20-year contractual requitement to transport one million tonnes of Nigerian liquefied natural gas annually to destinations worldwide.
The design uses the Moss catgo containment system, and has four independent 40m diameter spherical tanks installed within a double-skinned hull. The upper deck is flush, without forecastle, but has a sunken mooring deck aft. The tanks are covered by spirally-generated 250mm thick insulation, sheathed with aluminium foil sheet in order to protect the hull from brittle fractures, caused by contact with liquefied cargoes carried at -163°C, and to satisfy a low daily boil-off rate of 0.15% of cargo volume. A drip pan is fitted on the inner bottom to act as a pattial secondary barrier. That part of the tanks extending above the weather deck is especially heavily clad.

Cargo is loaded by shore pumps, into tanks that have _ been pre-cooled to the required temperature, and vapour generated during the operation, plus inert gas carried on ballast voyages, is returned to shore by means of the vessel's high-duty compressors. Boil-off gas generated during a loaded voyage is heated and delivered to the main boilers as fuel. Discharge is effected by two electric, 1500m?/h submerged pumps in each tank, with pressure in the tank maintained during the process by gas pumped in from shore, or received from the onboard LNG vapotiser. A cold environment and positive pressure is preserved in the tanks during ballast voyages by leaving a small amount of cargo on board.

An elevated walkway over the tanks provides access from the central control room (CCR), located below the wheelhouse, to the compressor room (between tanks 3 and 4, starboard side) and the foredeck. The CCR provides a central command station for cargo and ballasting operations, as well as monitoring the propulsion plant, integrated automation, and shipboard management systems.

A Hyundai-Mitsubishi steam turbine with high- pressute, low-pressure and astern units develops 23,160kW and is connected through a Mitsubishi double-
 reduction gearbox to a FP propeller for a service speed of
 19.75knots. Electrical power is supplied from two 3250kW turbine-driven alternators and two 1600kW diesel-driven sets. Steam is generated in two 55,000kg/h boilers burning heavy oil or boil-off gas.

  pallet-handling system

Shipbuilder:... China Shipbuilding Corp (Keelung yard), Taiwan
Vessel'S NAME!  t tesenes Luzon Strait
Aull NUMDET A.    seecseeesseserseevaes 730

 | Feesecsme the efficiency of refrigerated cargo handling under all weather conditions is something
 which has long occupied the minds of owners, operators
 and shippets of these cargoes, and specialist designer TTS-
Nortlift, together with Greek owner Enterprises Shipping
 & Trading (the original owner of this pair of ships), has devised an automated system using pallets/cages/trolleys and sideports, for this pair of reefers, which, it is claimed, can handle up to 7000 cartons of fruit houtly. The ship is flush decked with poop and long forecastle, and has machinery and superstructure right aft.

Four catgo holds ate arranged, and all decks are solid, with the second and fourth in Nos 2, 3, and 4 holds, and the second in No 1 hold, uninsulated. Cargo can be loaded
 in break-bulk or pallet form, or in containers on the
 weather deck, and three York refrigeration units operating
 on refrigerant R407C and CaCl, are used to cool the 15
 catgo compartments. These are arranged in eight independently insulated, gastight, temperature zones, and
 each has independent air circulation and fresh air supply.
One permanent conttolled-atmosphete (CA) system with micro-computer control of nitrogen gas is provided for the holds, and a modified atmosphere system (MA) setves the containers, 
The new TTS pallet-handling system operates through two large deckhouses positioned on the upper deck. Each is fitted with a pair of topside hatches, which can be worked by one of the two TTS-Norlift 45tonne swl deck cranes.
Side doors in the houses hinge outward and upward to provide cargo access, and at the same time give weather protection to the handling operation, with assistance from side curtains. They also support the extending rail and trolley which form the basis of the system.

Catgo is loaded into the ship using pallet-cages containing cartons, which ate hoisted by automated trolleys
 78
 (two in each deckhouse), radio-controlled by one man.
Within the vessel, distribution is by forklift truck. A feature of the system is the ability to programme pre-determined statting/stopping points for unloading and discharging, into the trolley operation. It also incorporates the facility for back-up from the deck ctanes, working through the hatch covers.

Luzon Strait is fitted with a Hyundai-MAN B&W 7S60MC-C main engine developing 21,490bhp MCR at 105rev/min and driving a FP propeller (with the high number of six blades) for a trial speed of 23knots. The results of noise and vibration measurement have satisfied the Det Norske Veritas criteria for catgo ship comfort tating number (crn) 3, when running ahead at 85% MCR on trial. Klectrical consumption is satisfied from four diesel- alternator sets, each developing 1500kW when running at 720tev/min.

MARITEA: 3 Maj-built product tanker for Italian owner

Shipbuilder:  Shipyard 3 Maj, Croatia
VeSSEl'S NAMEL     t tett s Maritea
HUI] NUMDES eee ec ccc   ee es nenaaees 682
Owner/operator: 0. Carichi Liquidi Societa
Azionaria SpA, Italy
DESIGNED... Shipyard 3-Maj, Croatia
Model test establishment used  Brodarski
Institut, Croatia
FAQ! o seseeusssseseevesesees ses ss Italy
Total number of sister ships already Completed: s reeen Nil
Total number of sister ships
Sit] me) 010) 40 [>] Sere 1

UILT with a single deck and forecastle, Maritea has been designed to carry oil products and IMO Type

II chemicals in six pairs of cargo tanks enclosed within
 a double hull. The structure of the cargo tanks is suitable for catgo densities of 1.025tonnes/m’ in completely filled tanks, and up to 1.54tonnes/m’ in partially filled tanks with the corresponding to the density ratio. The cargo spaces
 ate divided by centreline and transverse, vertically troughed bulkheads, and stiffened by longitudinal
 framing placed outside of the tanks, which are
 protected by a phenolic epoxy coating, Each pair of
 catgo tanks has an independent pipeline to the cross- over manifold, and a stern-loading station is provided.

Handling cargo is by means of 12 Framo submerged pumps, each moving 500m’*/h to give a total discharge rate of around 4000m°’/h. A common high-pressure hydraulic system operates from six power packs and allows eight pumps to run simultaneously. Cargo heating uses thermal oil, and can raise the temperature from 44°C to 66°C in 96hours. Cargo operations are served by venting and inert-gas systems and vapour emission control, tank-cleaning using Scanjet fixed machines, two Framo tank-cleaning pumps, and an
Aalborg heater. Saab TankRadar level, temperature, and inert-gas pressure gauges are fitted, together with high level and overflow alarms, and cargo operations are masterminded from a control room in the

SIGNIFICANT SHIPS OF 2002
 filling height
 accommodation block.

The main engine is a Sulzer 6RTA52U design, built by the shipyard's engine department, and developing 9000kW MCR at 130rev/min. The CP propeller is from
Rolls-Royce Kamewa, and the three diesel-generator sets each produce 1120kW. A central cooling system serves the machinery installation. A Becker high- performance flap-type rudder and Rolls-Royce
Kamewa 1000kKW bow thruster enhance manoeuvrability, and a single-point bow mooring system is provided. Emergency towing facilities are arranged forward and aft.

Accommodation for 26 permanent crew is provided aft, berthed in 24 single and one double cabin, with a six-berth room available for a Suez or repair crew. A free-fall lifeboat operates over the stern, and a rescue boat and liferafts are also carried.
