MONT ST MICHEL: flagship ferry provides increased capacity on popular link

Shipbuilder: vVan der Giessen-de Noord BV, The Netherlands

VeSSEl'S NAME?  0c   ee ee  s Mont St Michel
Hull MUMDES:.. wee     ea eee ea ee en  s 985
Owner/operator:  Brittany Ferries, France
Designer:.. Leese . Deltamarin, Finland
Model test establishment used  MARIN, The Netherlands eC cc France
Total number of sister ships already completed:  eee e es Nil
Total number of sister ships
S110) 01 ©) (0 (>) oe Nil

TARTING with Via Ligure (Significant Ships of
 1992), Van der Giessen-de Noord has developed a fine reputation as a builder of to-pax ferries, now extended to include this vessel, built for her owner's overnight Portsmouth/Caen service. The design closely follows that of Turku-built consort Normandie (also featured in Significant Ships of 1992), with
Finnish consultant Deltamarin involved with both vessels. In line with her builder's thinking, there are no barriers fitted within the vehicle spaces: instead, B/5 longitudinal bulkheads in the lower garage deck, and tight transverse sub-division, together with a suitable beam and ample freeboard, are claimed to ensure the required level of damage stability and compliance, as specified by the Stockholm
Agreement.

Mont St Michel is expected to provide an extra 70% freight capacity on her scheduled route, and to offer 20% more car capacity, utilising 2250 lane metres on three fixed decks, plus 1180 metres on a hoistable platform deck, to carry 903 cars and 132 trucks. Main access is at main deck (No 3) level over a single- section door/ramp at the stern, and a three-section ramp at the bow, behind which an internal door is fitted in an arrangement closed by a clam-type bow visor. In addition, the upper deck level (No 5) has direct access from shore linkspans through a sliding weathertight door in the superstructure, forward, and an open end, aft.

Internal movements are by way of fixed ramps with watertight covers at each end of the space, which link
No 3 deck with the lower garage, and a tilting ramp connecting the main deck with upper levels. The hoistable deck is served by ramps formed from the panels.

Decks 7, 8, 9 and 10, plus three sun decks, accommodate the 2120 passengers, 788 of whom are carried in 224 cabins. Four disabled berths are also available and reclining-seat lounges arranged. A 90-
 86
 seat restaurant expressly for use by truck drivers supplements the a la carte and self-service facilities offered to ordinary passengers. Other amenities include lounges and a piano bar, two cinemas, disco, children's playroom, teenagers area, and shops.
Lifesaving equipment comprises 4 x 400-passenger marine escape systems, 4 x 150-person lifeboats, liferafts, and rescue boats. Passenger comfort is enhanced by the use of an Intering anti-heeling system and a pair of Brown Brothers fin stabilisers.

The machinery installation is a conventional four- engine/twin-screw configuration using Caterpillar
Mak 6M43 units linked in pairs through two geatboxes to Rolls-Royce Kamewa CP propellers.
Power take-off shafts from the gearboxes drive two 2200kW alternators designed to cope with normal sea loads and to supply two 1500kW bow thrusters whilst manoeuvring. Three 1620kW diesel-alternator sets are also fitted.

MURILLO: more expansion by state-owned
Spanish ferry operator

Shipbuilder:  IZAR (Seville yard), Spain
VESSEI'S NAMEL  ccc  n senens Murillo
Hull NUMDES ccc  seesensteeeseetneens 291
Owner/operator:  Trasmediterranea SA, Spain
DESIQNED t n s IZAR, Spain
Model test establishment used:  MARIN, The Netherlands
FLAG! eee  s sssssseesevsnseesenntereesenenees Spain
Total number of sister ships already completed:  Nil (but variants to
 basis design for other owners)
Total number of sister ships

SCANT investment by Spanish state-owned ferry operator Trasmediterranea over recent years has seen the introduction of high-class tonnage for the company's southern Spain/Balearics services. For this latest addition to its fleet, the company has taken advantage of builder IZAR's successful standard ferry design to develop a vessel, variously described as 'the largest vessel in her owner's fleet’, and as 'the largest to- pax under the Spanish flag’, 
On a hull form first introduced by the shipbuilder in 1998 for Cenargo (Dawn Merchant Significant Ships of 1998), modifications have been made which have seen 66 extta passenger cabins added to the design, also the provision of 1900/400lane metres of truck/car space, respectively, available on four decks. This is a large inctease when compared with Dawn Merchant, which offers just 2000m on two decks.

A major change from the Cenargo series is the deletion of bow loading, with access on to the main deck now made only over a stern door/ramp. The previously unused lower hold has been adapted to carry vehicles, with fixed ramps linking the two car decks in that space and connecting with the main deck. Tiltable, hoistable tamps either side of the engine casing transfer freight between the main and upper decks, and form watertight closures when stowed flush against the main deck. The access equipment is of MacGregor design, and this company has also supplied the four jalousie-type control doots that provide the capability to withstand flooding on the main vehicle deck to a depth of 0.5m. When open, the doors stow parallel to the deckhead.

The machinery installation remains substantially as for the basis vessel, and features the same four Wartsila 9L38 main engines, each developing 5940kW at 600rev/min and attanged in pairs in separate enginerooms. A pair of
Reintjes twin-input/single-output gearboxes are fitted, with the main drive to twin Wattsila (Lips) stainless steel ptopellers. A power take-off from each gearbox connects
 88
 with a Leroy Somer 1400kW alternator, with three
Wartsila-driven 1200kW diesel-driven sets also fitted. Two
Brunvoll 1300kW bow thrusters aid manoeuvrability. An
Inteting active-tank heel control system is fitted, with passenger comfort further enhanced by a pair of
Mitsubishi retractable fin stabilisers.

NEPTUNE AEGLI: vehicle
 carrier for Mediterranean
 trades

Shipbuilder: 0.00. Hijos de J Barreras SA (Astillero Barreras), Spain

VESSEl'S NAME...  s st s Neptune Aegli 0] | 01000] >) Oe 1594

PTUNE AEGLI, and sister ship Neptune Dynamis, are two more examples of the Vigo shipyard's new- found success in the pure vehicle carrier market (Szgnificant Ships of 1999/2001). They represent the first newbuildings in the Greek-owned Neptune fleet, whose principal interest is the distribution of new vehicles and components between assembly plants, and to export markets within the Mediterranean. Designed with a service speed of 20knots - sufficient to maintain a weekly return voyage between Spain and Turkey, Neptune Aegh has seven full-length vehicle decks, two of which (4 and 6) are hoistable and can be positioned at varying heights between their respective fixed decks. When stowed, these allow adequate headroom on the main and upper decks (3 and 5) for the vessel to load 87 trailers and 470 carts.
Carrying cats alone, total capacity is 1500.

Vehicle entry is at No 3 deck, over an axial ramp/door in the stern, which is capable of supporting the simultaneous load of two road trailers totalling 85 tonnes, and a secondary ramp/door in the starboard quarter, with a loading of 45tonnes. A fixed ramp with end-hinged cover links the port side of the main deck (3) with the lower hold, and a hoistable ramp on the centreline serves the main and upper decks. A second hoistable ramp is arranged between No 6 deck and the upper deck (7), forming a non-watertight closure in that deck when not in use. Access to hoistable decks (4 and 6) is by means of ramps in the deck panels.
 90

Operation of the cargo equipment is carried out hydraulically, with rams, pulleys, wires and jigger winches, and loading/discharging of vehicles is facilitated by the fitting of an Intering anti-heeling system. The vehicle spaces have been structurally designed without the use of pillars, thereby reducing the potential for cargo damage, and the hull incorporates a double-skin format up to hoistable car deck level (Deck 4).

Two Wartsila 8L46B main engines are fitted, each developing 7800kW at 500rev/min and connected to a
Rolls-Royce shafting and propeller package through a
Reintjes gearbox, each of which drives a 1200kW alternator. Two 990kW diesel-alternator sets ate also provided, and ship control is by means of a Kongsberg
Maritime Databridge 1028 automation system, linked with an integrated navigation installation. Two 1000kW thrusters are fitted at the bow to aid manoeuvring. A single-tier deckhouse on the boat deck (8) accommodates a crew of 25 plus 12 drivers, and this is surmounted at the forward end by the full-width wheelhouse.

NORGAS ORINDA: first Norgas
 ‘super-cooler’ enters service

Shipbuilder: 0 s Hudong-Zhonghua

Shipbuilding (Group) Ltd, China

VESSEI'S NAMEN cisisiivccavuwasevivens Norgas Orinda

HUI] MUMDBES?..00. oo. cee  seneeuneees 429

Owner/Operator’...  s reners Somargas Ltd
 (| M Skaugen ASA)/Norgas, Norway

DEON  iscenawwix Carl Bro A/S-Dwinger

Marineconsult, Denmark

FAQs sss seusvevcerses nueeseveeuuunsevees Hang Kong

Total number of sister ships

 2 larger units and 4 options, size
 unknown

HIS new series of innovative gas carriers has been introduced specifically to improve the service
Norgas provides for its customers, particularly by reducing port turn-around-time. To accomplish this, Tractebel Gas Engineering, Germany, sub-supplier of the gas plant, developed an installation in which IMO tank pressure has been increased from 4.5bar to 7bar and, in conjunction with the use of maximum cooling capacity, quoted as 2°C/day in tropical waters, loading time for ambient propylene at 25°C has been cut by two/three days, compared with that of similar conventional vessels, whilst between four and 10 days can be saved when cooling a cargo from 25"C to -46'C.
By adopting a 'make-it-simple' philosophy, the designers have developed a tank and piping arrangement for easy operation, which ensures that in any loading condition critical stability conditions are never reached. Cargo processing equipment Is automated, and controlled from the bridge, along with ballasting and operation of the main engine.

This ‘super-cooler’ design features a shallow-draught and wide-beam arrangement that allows the vessel to load and discharge on an even keel, and to take on a large cargo in shallow-water ports. Manoeuvrability obtained from the high-efficiency flap rudder and bow thruster enables the vessel to track sideways and turn in its own length, thereby contributing to the fast turn- around required.

Within a double-skin hull, whose side tanks are used for water ballast, there are two cargo tanks of 5322m’ and 3139m’ capacity, respectively, their cylindrical shape with spherical ends offering 30% less surface area than bi-lobe designs. In addition, a cylindrical 90m’ cryogenic tank 1s installed on the upper deck for the carriage of coolant, with a vaporising system enabling a quick 'gassing-up' process. A nitrogen production plant is also fitted, allowing the vessel to purge its tanks on route, and, in combination with the deck tank, to carry out a full change of grade whilst at

SIGNIFICANT SHIPS OF 2002
 sea: in both cases arriving at a terminal ready for loading, Discharge of LPG without the need for the use of water as a heat transfer fluid is made possible by the inclusion of a thermal oil heating system.

Norgas Orinda is fitted with a MAN B&W 6L48/60 main engine, developing 6300kW MCR and driving a
CP propeller through a gearbox which has a power take-off for a 1700kW alternator. The arrangement is designed to operate In reverse - as a ‘power take-in' as part of a fully redundant, 'take me home’ main engine installauon, where power from the auxiliary engines is directed back through the gearbox to provide limited propulsion in an emergency. Additional electrical supply is derived from four 550kW diesel alternators.

PERTINACIA: Italian-built and -owned
 cable laying/repair ship

Shipbuilder:... Cantiere Navale Fratelli Orlando, 
Italy
Vessel'S MAMEL ssecs ssees Pertinacia
Hull NUMBERS...    ses seeenes nss s 281
Owner/operator: 0..c0. Elettra, SpA, Italy
DESIGN sisicvssscscinaricavereased Vik-Sandvik, Norway

HE rapid expansion of underwater, fibre-optic cable links has seen a growing demand for speedy vessels to lay new networks worldwide, and to maintain them in service (although unfortunately the market taday is severely diminished as a result of over- confidence in this sector). As Significant Ships has shown
 over recent years, the adoption of a fully stern-working
 concept to carry out these duties has proved very successful, with the offshore supply vessel (OSV) configuration providing the ideal platform.

As a leading designer of that type of vessel, Vik-Sandvik has been able to move easily into this new
 mode, and Pertinaca is an example of the suitability of this ubiquitous design for such work. The attraction of
 the OSV is the long, continuous aft deck, and when this is virtually completely covered by a long forecastle, an ideal workshop area is the result. On top of this, a superstructure accommodating 75 personnel has been built, with control of all navigation and cable operations carried out from a large wheelhouse positioned above this, forward of midships.

Pertinacia is equipped to handle any type of underwater tclecommunication cable from l4mm to 150mm diameter, complete with repeaters up to 400mm diameter. Onboard cable storage 1s provided in two tanks having a total capacity of around 6000tonnes. ‘Two smaller tanks increase that by some 400tonnes and can be used to hold recovered cable. Arrangements have been made for the use of modern equipment and technology, for cable burying by plough, and ROV operation, also buoy deployment and retrieval.

The cable-handling system consists of one 4m diameter cable-drum engine, one 20tonne linear cable engine with 20 wheel pairs, and one 6tonne draw-off/hold-back unit with six wheel pairs. All are capable of local or bridge control. A 140tonne Hydralift traction winch and a storage winch are fitted on the weather deck, whilst a 6Otonne plough-handling A-frame, also from Hydralift, operates over the stern. A helideck on the forecastle can host a 19.5m helicopter.

A diesel-electric propulsion system is fitted, using two electrically driven steerable thrusters under the stern.
These are powered from four 3750kKVA alternator sets with 3240kW Wartsila 8R32 diesel prime movers, which
 ensure a service speed whilst in repair mode of
 14.50knots, and approximately 4knots when laying, A bollard pull of 100tonnes is attainable. Together with two side thrusters and a retractable thruster forward, the

 system presents a arrangement with
 fully
 station
 dynamically keeping and
 transponders,

  france telecom
 ship for French service

Shipbuilder: 068 Hanjin Heavy Industries & Construction Co Ltd, Korea

HIS larger version of Segero, Hanjin's first cable
 ship (Significant Ships of 1998), has been designed to lay, bury, and repair intercontinental submarine fibre-optic cable, in severe weather conditions without tug assistance, in compliance with a specification which calls for a forward bollard pull in sea state 6 of 65tonnes against a 2knot current, and with a 25knot square beam wind.

The general arrangement conforms to the fully stern-working concept, which is now accepted practice for this class of vessel, and allows work to be carried out in the worst conditions. It also permits a conventional bow form with consequent higher cruising speed.

All cable work is concentrated on a continuous main deck, almost totally enclosed by a long forecastle which forms the upper deck, and uses port and starboard cable lines. Each of these is fitted with a 4m diameter/40tonne-pull drum engine with a two-motor
AC electric drive combination, and a 20-wheel pair draw-off/hold-back linear cable engine. This equipment, together with a two-wheel-pair cable transporter, has been supplied by Dowty Handling
Systems, UK.

The package is designed to lay cable at speeds up to 6knots over two 3m-diameter stern sheaves. These are straddled by a 40tonne swl A-frame which is capable of working in an arc ranging from 80deg over the stern to 70deg inboard, and provides for launching and recovering a cable plough from either cable line.
The working area is also served by a 5tonne articulated jib crane and a 3tonne telescopic jib unit, with a 2tonne fixed jib crane forward.

Elsewhere on this deck there are test rooms and workshops, and three repeater stacks spray cooled by chilled fresh water. These are fitted over the three main cable tanks of total capacity 3050m° (5250tonnes). An additional 200m’ cable tank is atranged forward of these main tanks.

Accommodation for 75 persons is concentrated in a deckhouse block built on the upper deck, with space forward and aft of this available for stowage of buoys, and modular units used for cable plough control and
ROV operations. The extensive navigation bridge is given over to a combined wheelhouse/control centre with full-width view forward and aft. An Alstom integrated monitoring and control system is fitted, incorporating dynamic positioning, functions related

SIGNIFICANT SHIPS OF 2002
 to cable laying and trenching, joystick operation of thrusters, and power management. An STN Atlas total navigation system is also provided.

The diesel-electric power system is based on four
Alstom 3900kW alternators, plus a smaller, 970kW auxiliary set, all driven by Wartsila diesel engines.
Power is distributed through two switchboards and four transformers to two Alstom 3916kW propulsion motots driving twin shafts with open-type FP propellers. Two Rolls-Royce Kamewa 1500kW tunnel thrusters ate fitted at each end of the ship, with an additional 1500kW retractable azimuth thruster at the bow. All propulsion and thruster motors ate speed- controlled by AC frequency converters that provide immediate response for manoeuvring purposes; they ate assisted by two high-lift, flap-type rudders.

ROMANTIKA: versatile ferry for Gulf of
Finland service

Shipbuilder:  Aker Finnyards OY, Finland
VeSSEl'S NAME...   eee Romantika

LTHOUGH the use of so large and luxurious a
 ferry as Romanttka on the three-and-a-half-hour run between Tallinn and Helsinki, might seem extravagant at first glance, justification for its size lies in the increasing demand within the Gulf of Finland for mini-cruise and seaborne conference facilities, developing as part of Estonia's expanding economy.
This burgeoning trade is catered for on the new vessel (which offers a 50% increase in both passenger capacity and vehicle stowage space over existing ferries on the service), by two decks (6 and 7) devoted solely to 'cruising distraction’, and designed by four specialist interior architects.

The principal feature is a two-deck high, 1000-seat show lounge right aft, complemented by five restaurants seating 3140 customers, in the forward parts of those decks. The usual lounges and children's play areas are also available, with 1000m’ of supermarket and boutique space a major attraction.

Conference facilities on deck 9 comprise 11 rooms of various sizes and a sauna suite, whilst a total of 727 two- and four-berth en-suite passenger cabins providing 2172 beds, are arranged on decks 5, 8 and 9. Crew accommodation is positioned on decks 9, and 10, where the full-width navigating bridge is located.
Three MacGregor 16-person passenger lifts link the various decks.

Lifesaving appliances include six marine evacuation stations, and there are also six enclosed lifeboats and a full complement of liferafts. Passenger access is through forward and aft side doors, P&S, on to deck 5, with main reception on deck 6.

As built, Romantika has only one vehicle deck (No 3), but there is scope for adding platform decks at a later stage. Currently 1000lane metres of vehicle stowage are available, equivalent to around 300 cars, laid out with three lanes to port, and four to starboard, of the central casing. Access is over the bow and stern, with twin ramp/doors aft, and a single bow entry which incorporates a unique folding frame ramp/door designed by TTS (the Norwegian company which recently acquired the cargo access division of Hamworthy KSE), forming part of the bow access arrangements. All three ramps are rated for trailers up to 52.5tonnes total weight and a maximum 40ft tractor/trailer weight of 60tonnes.

SIGNIFICANT SHIPS OF 2002

The machinery installation makes use of four
Wartsila 16V32 main engines each developing 6560kW at 750rev/min, driving two CP propellers at 141rev/min through two twin-input/single-output geatboxes. Power take-offs from the gearboxes connect with two 2700kVA alternators, with supplementary electrical supply derived from three
Wartsila/Leroy Somer 2960kVA diesel sets. Two 1800kW bow thrusters are fitted for manoeuvring, and passenger comfort is aided by Mitsubishi stabiliser fins and Frank Mohn anti-heeling tanks.

SG AFMARINE CUNEME

SHIPDUIICEL:. cc   SSW Fahr-und
Spezialschiffoau GmbH, Germany
Vessel'S NAME)...   Safmarine Cunene (launched as ER Bremerhaven)
HUI] NUMDES      setssss s senes 2002
Owner/operator: E R Schiffahrt GmbH & Co, Germany/Safmarine, South Africa
Designer:  SSW Fahr-und Spezialschiffbau
GmbH, Germany
Model test establishment used  Hamburgische
Versuchsanstalt (HSVA), Germany
FLAG! eee  ssssees seesssssueeesenssv s Liberia
Total number of sister ships already completed:  Nil
Total number of sister ships
STII] ON OOP...    ssss sesn nn 6

Res owiNG a period of ferry building and conversions (Szguificant Ships of 2007), the former
Schichau-Seebeck shipyard at Bremerhaven, now trading under the SSW banner, has switched its interests to a new design of container ship, marketed as the SSW Super 25 (with the numeral reflecting its nominal 2500TEU capacity), and now successfully launched with the aid of this large order from Hamburg-based ER Schiffahrt.
Backing by charters from Safmatine and Maersk meant that a tight delivery schedule had to be imposed on the builder; consequently SSW subcontracted construction of the hulls of three vessels in the series to Daewoo Magnalia, in Romania. However, fitting out of all the ships is being carried out at Bremerhaven.

Today, a feature of many container ship designs is the increase in the numbers of refrigerated containers which can be carried, particularly out of Australasia, and Safmarine
Cunene provides electrical connections for 362 reefer units, with 142 of these located in the holds. A significant number of containers loaded with IMO hazardous cargoes can be accommodated on deck and in the holds, along with high- cube and other non-standard sizes. It is also of note that container-stowage atrangements include the positioning of securing points between two 20ft units stowed in a 40ft slot.

This to meet Australian requirements, in furtherance of proposed deployment on South Africa/Australia routes.
The SSW Super 25 series is double-hulled, and has a forecastle fitted with breakwater, also a poop on which a six-tier deckhouse is erected, surmounted by a navigating bridge with offset conning position. The vessels are designed to be self-sustaining, with three MacGregor GL type slim-line 45tonne swl cranes positioned between cargo hatches closed by pontoon covers.

The machinery installation follows conventional lines, with a Sulzer 7RTA72U-B main engine fitted aft, and producing 21,500kW at 99rev/min, directly coupled to a
FP propeller to give a service speed of 22knots when running at 90% MCR and allowing a 15% sea margin. The hull form is a new one, developed at the HSVA tank, 
SIGNIFICANT SHIPS OF 2002

Hamburg, to attain that speed, which is slightly higher than normal for this size and type of vessel. Electrical supply comes from three 2240kW diesel-alternator sets.

ST APOSTLE ANDREW: new series revives Russian merchant shipbuilding

SHIBDUNGER i ssawovesneres: Severnaya Verf, Russia
Vessel'S NAMe@: s ns ot Apastle Andrew
ALA HITS, cnscovanonvgiaraieaansaepetivarsbne tna 901
Owner/operator:  North West Shipping Co, Russia
DONG wsscsisouwcox. Vympel (Design Office for
Shipbuilding), Russia
FRED cascas conan semeamnsqewrmmsnxcanienenastoatgactan Russia
Total number of sister ships aieady COMPIGIOG  siaccedas sicker ccaauesheiees Nil
Total number of sister ships still on order:  9 (plus 4 for other owners)

HE vast extent of Russia's navigable canals, rivers and lakes, allows cargo to be transported from virtually every inland port in the country to river terminals throughout Europe, and to the open sea. This trade has resulted in the development, over many years, of
 massive specialised river/sea cargo ship designs, mainly identified by their long and narrow configuration. Many of these ships are now elderly, and a new series of this unique ship-type, known as the Valday class (also designated
Project OLOLO), now under construction at the St
Petersburg yard of Severnaya, will, it is hoped, serve to introduce a new era into Russian shipbuilding.

Despite their characteristic proportions, the river/sea designs have proved acceptable for
International trading over the years, to the extent that the Severnaya shipyard is currently developing a single-screw version of the Valday class for sea-only
 merchant
 service. S/ Apostle Andrew features three long, large holds, closed by MacGregor Foldtite hatch covers built upon a high coaming, A fore and aft gangway runs between forecastle and poop decks to provide a working platform at this level, and also acts as protection for the access passage at upper deck level.

The double-hull structure ensures box-shaped cargo suitable for range of general commodities carried in bulk or sacks, as well as
 spaces a wide packaged and loose timber, palletised vegetables, steel coils up to 35tonnes weight, paper reels, and 267TEL, up to 20 of which can be refrigerated. No cargo gear is fitted, and the folding signal mast forward contributes to a long, open and flat, cargo stowage area on top of the hatch covers.

A twin-serew machinery installation is based upon a pair of Wartsilii 6L20C main engines, built at the designet's Finnish works. Each of these develops
LO80kW and drives an FP propeller through a Reintjes reduction gearbox. A rudder is fitted behind each propeller and a 150kW tunnel-type thruster operates at the bow. Electrical power is derived from three
 104

Caterpillar diesel-driven 160kW alternator sets.
Emphasising the probable dual role of the vessels, Sf Apostle Andrew carries a free-fall lifeboat at the stern, whilst another prominent feature common to
Russian vessels 1s a stern anchor and winch.

STENA BRITANNICA the European ro-pax market

Shipbuilder:  Hyundai Heavy Industries
Co Ltd, Korea
VeSSEl'S NAMEL  ccc  s Siena Britannica 10] | 010/00] 0)~) ore 1392
Owner/operator: 6.. Stena Ro-Ro, Sweden
Designer:  Hyundai Heavy Industries Co Ltd, Korea
FAQ!  ssssssseeeuseeea seteeeserenns UK
Total number of sister ships already COMPIEte” sssss s Nil

Sti] OM OFF...  ses s uesenesneeses 1

TENA BRITANNICA forms an important
 reference for the Hyundai yard, marking an entry into the European ro-pax sector that has been the aim of the company for some time. The design is based on that of the smaller Seapacer class, built at the Puerto
Real shipyard of Astilleros Espafioles some three years ago (Finnchpper, Significant Ships of 1999), and the vessel has entered service on Stena's Harwich/Hook of Holland route as a replacement for a two-year old unit from that earlier class, and previously bearing the same name.

Of particular note is the availability of almost 3500 lane metres of vehicle stowage on four fixed decks (Nos 1, 3, 5, 7), and one hoistable deck, positioned at the starboard side of No 5 deck. Vehicles are loaded and unloaded through wide bow and _ stern ramp/doots on No 3 deck, and two internal fixed ramps with side-hinged watertight covers take vehicles to deck 1. A tiltable ramp links the entry deck with deck 5, the stern part of which, together with the
 exposed part of deck 7, is classified for the carriage of
 dangerous cargoes.

The passenger reception area is on deck 7, reached either through side doors P&S, or by stairs and lifts from the vehicle decks. This deck also provides the
Globetrotter's Café, buffet restaurant, shops, and driver's lounge. A cafeteria seating 350, and the ‘children's place’ are on deck 8, half of which is allocated to passenger cabins. Together with deck 9, this level provides 101 two-berth outside cabins, 88 two-betth and 55 four-berth, inside, cabins, contributing to a total complement of 900 passengers.
A 66-seat cinema, and a conference room ate also located on deck 9, with crew accommodation on deck 10 and a fully enclosed wheelhouse, forward on deck 11 (this arrangement is to be slightly modified in the later sister ship).

Cargo access equipment is supplied by MacGregor, and includes the use for the first time with a newbuilding, of Corex, stainless steel, sandwich panels, a product developed by the company particularly for hoistable decks and ramps, where its low weight and profile is advantageous. Also included in the MacGregor package are passenger and service elevators, a 500ke dumb waiter, and nine cold rooms operating at a range of temperatures.
 106
 a0 LA OL
® * i tb it ve r

Stena Britannica is fitted with four MAN B&W 9L40/54 main engines, each developing 6480kW at 500rev/min and driving twin CP propellers through a pait of twin-input/single-output gearboxes for a service speed of 22knots. Two bow thrusters and a hinged-flap rudder are fitted. Electrical requirements ate satisfied from two 2400kW alternators powered from the gearboxes, and four 1280kW diesel-driven
 sets.

STENA CARIBBEAN |

STENA CARIBBEAN: the C-Max
 innovation

Shipbuilder  Stocznia Gdynia SA, Poland
VeSSEl'S NAME...  s Stena Caribbean
Hull MUMDEF on...  sesteenees 8226/1
Owner/operator:  ees Stena Ltd, UK
Designer:  Stocznia Gdynia SA, Poland
Model test establishment used: CTO, Poland [a0 rr Bermuda
Total number of sister ships

Already COMPIEtEd: s s s Nil
Total number of sister ships
 1] 0) 91k @) 0 (>) Sore 1

LONG-TERM charter arrangement, made by Stena
 with Texaco Eastern Caribbean for two 10,000dwt oil products/LPG tankers, led to the development of this complex design of wide-beam/shallow-draught vessel, whose breadth is 23.80m compared with a mote usual value for this size of vessel of between 17m/19m. Of particular importance to the design, which owes much to the concept of the much larger VLCC Sena Victory (see
Significant Ships of 2007), is the ship's planned trading area, which includes operating amongst the many Caribbean islands, and loading and discharging in restricted waters, even direct to beaches using floating hose pipes.

The varied types of cargo to be carried has also resulted in a configuration of 16 integral coated tanks, plus a draining tank on deck, all of which can function as cargo tanks, offering 17 double-valve segregations and a cattying capacity of 12,540m°. In addition, there are two 640m’ fully pressurised LPG tanks with associated handling equipment fitted on deck. The underdeck tanks ate smooth sided, framed externally, treated with epoxy coatings, and fitted with coils. Framo hydraulic submerged pumps ensure that a maximum discharge of 1800m°/h can be achieved, using an array of 14 manifolds amidships.

Stena has opted for a four-generator diesel-electric ptopulsion system, with its advantages of space-saving and flexibility, and where the combination of two large and two small units allows power delivery to be closely matched with demand. The installation comprises two 6R32LND and two 4L20C models, both from the
Wartsila stable, each driving Siemens alternators of 2250kW and 630kW output respectively, to supply power.
The largest individual demand is from the two Rolls-
Royce 2200kW azimuthing propeller-thrusters, fitted with frequency-controlled, variable-speed actuation.

This multi-engine/multi-genset layout promotes trading dependability, safety and redundancy, and assists maintenance by allowing the vessel to continue operations whilst individual units are shut down. The manoeuvring qualities offered by the 360deg propulsors ate enhanced by a bow thruster supplied from another arm of the expanding Rolls-Royce group, whilst power management is one element of an integrated automation system implemented by Siemens, general supplier of the electrical ptopulsion plant. This is arranged within three

SIGNIFICANT SHIPS OF 2002

NN
 operational modes (sea passage, manoeuvting, and discharge), all masterminded from either engine control room, ot wheelhouse.

SUNBELT SPIRIT: unique ‘two-way’ trader from Sumitomo

ShHIPDUIIGELr:    e eect e ees Sumitomo Heavy

Industries Ltd, Japan

VESSEl'S NAMEL s s Sunbelt Spirit

Owner/operator:  Great American Lines Inc,

Designer  Sumitomo Heavy Industries Ltd,

 a (c\0 Republic of the Marshall Islands
Total number of sister ships

 'S}t|] te) 8 ©) (6 (=| cre Nil

PERHAPS long-overdue refinement to the car- carrier concept has been introduced into its North
Pacific services by Great American Lines. Faced with uneconomic return ballast voyages following deliveries of cars from Japan to USA, this operator developed the attractive idea of incorporating refrigerated space into the design of Sawnbelt Spirit, enabling the vessel to load some 9500m’ of refrigerated cargo into eight designated compartments, arranged within the car space.
The refrigerating plant installed for this diversification is capable of maintaining temperatures in the range +15°C to -25°C, using a direct-expansion system with

Refrigerant 404A, and is suitable for the transport of
 cargoes such as fruit, bananas, and frozen goods. The
 installation has been designed for the future fitting of
 controlled atmosphere (CA) equipment, should this be required. For loading the reefer cargoes, four Tsuji 4tonne swl trolley hoists have been fitted, operating through sideports on the starboard side of the hull.

In car-carrying mode, Sunbelt Spirit uses two
MacGregor-Kayaba starboard side-loading ramps at the stern and amidships, to handle vehicles which, once on board, are distributed over 12 cargo decks, and one garage deck, using a series of fixed ramps. In all 54,000m?’ of deck space is available for stowing 6190 vehicles.

Sunbelt Spirit's service speed of 21.00knots is higher than that of many similar vessels, and has necessitated a larger engine than most, a Diesel United-built Sulzer 8RTA62U unit with an MCR output of 17,760kW at 113rev/min being the selection. Electrical power is derived from three Daihatsu/Taiyo diesel-alternator sets, each of 1300kW capacity, with their output serving the requirements of the refrigeration plant and a 1500kW bow thruster.

HE design of Tarantella closely follows that of

Agov Sea, introduced in the late 1990s by the Trogir
Shipyard, and presented in Szgnificant Ships of 1998.

Principal particulars differ only slightly from those of
 that earlier vessel, however, a number of improvements have been made to the design, including extending the double-skin hull aft into the engineroom, to protect the bunker tanks. The side, and centrally divided bottom spaces, ate combined to form water ballast spaces surrounding the seven pairs of cargo tanks, and two slop tanks (instead of five and and two tanks, respectively, in Azov Sea). They are all arranged with angled corner connections and have tank bottoms slightly sloped to the centreline, in compliance with
DNV notation 'str 0.05', to facilitate drainage of cargo and washing water.

An interesting feature of the design is the use of
 trapezoidal, in place of square, corrugations in the tank structure, in order to allow 96% of the surface to be exposed to direct-jet washing water, to qualify for an
ETC notation. A capability for handling IMO Grades I as well as III chemicals in this modified design, has led to the strengthening of bulkhead and boundary structure of alternate tanks for an unlimited filling level of 1.60 tonnes/m® cargo density. The deck structure is also reinforced to withstand 0.70bar overpressure, thus preventing cargoes such as pentane and pentene from vaporising at environmental temperatures.

A review of the cargo tank and double hull structure by 3D FEM fatigue-life analysis as part of the DNV
Nauticus (Newbuilding) assessment, followed by the application of extra thickness, brackets or cuts-out, as required for DNV PLUS-2 notation, has introduced a structure fatigue life of 40 years. It has also been
 decided to use a high-tensile steel (NV36) content of
 31.5% in the tank envelope and bottom plating in order to reduce the steel hull mass.

The Tarantella class has been fitted with a modified cargo handling system based upon electrically driven deepwell pumps, controlled by frequency converters, whilst tank drying is accomplished by means of a deck- mounted 30,000m°/h air heater, with steel ducting and flexible connections to the required tanks.

Two Mak 8M32C main engines are fitted in a machinery space right aft. Each develops 3840kW at 600rev/min and drives a single CP propeller through a twin-input/single-output gearbox which can be de- clutched to allow one engine only to power the ship. A

TENERIFE CAR:

Barreras), Spain

VESSEl'S NAMEL  Tenerife Car

FU] NUMDE|T eee  s ssts senennes 1591

Ti Vigo shipyard of Barreras continues to benefit from its growing expertise as a builder of pute vehicle carriers, mainly for use in transporting new cars and assemblies between the many subsidiary factoties of international manufacturers, in and around Southern Europe. Tenerife Car continues an association with Spanish owner Vapores Suardiaz which has been apparent, in one way or another, in all the ships of this type so fat completed by the shipyard, and this latest design owes much to that of Gran
Canaria Car (Significant Ships of 2001), which Suardiaz
Opetates on charter, whilst including many improvements dictated by the service life of that earlier vessel.

Perhaps the most important of these changes are a change in freeboard (main) deck from the higher No 4 level to the mote conventional No 3 deck, also the inclusion of a second, hoistable vehicle deck, within an 8-deck arrangement accessed over a stetn ramp/door at No 3 deck. This ramp can accept the simultaneous loading of two toad trailers totalling 38tonnes.
Supplementary loading is handled through a starboard side ramp/door to No 5 deck, positioned in front of the deckhouse.

A fixed ramp with watertight closure leads from the main deck to the tank top, and three moveable ramps join decks 4 and 5; 6 and 7; 7 and 8. The two hoistable decks are designated 2 and 4, and are accessed from ramps in the hoistable sections. When these decks are stowed, the clear height allows long trailers to be catried on the tank top and main deck. A total of 3684lane mettes is available for 1356 cars: alternatively, with 825m allocated for trailers, 794 cars can be carried, 
The main engine specification has been changed from that of Gran Canaria Car, however, the selection of two Wartsila 9L38 units is an endorsement of
Suardiaz's choice for that earlier vessel, of machinery from the engine builder's 'pipeless' range, then recently introduced, in which all water and oil ducts are

SIGNIFICANT SHIPS OF 2002
 incorporated in the engine block. The larger-bore engines now fitted each develop 6525kW at 600trev/min, and drive Rolls-Royce shaft lines and propellers through a pait of Reintjes gearboxes, for a service speed of 20.00knots. Also built-into the installation is a Wartsila
Engine Control System (WECS).

A power take-off from each gearbox connects with a
Stamford 875kW alternator, with two
Caterpillar/Stamford 650kW diesel-driven sets also available. The full-length superstructure, which encloses the vehicle spaces, is surmounted, aft of midships, by a
 two-tier deckhouse plus wheelhouse, which accommodates the crew of 17, and 12 drivers.

THE WORLD: introducing a new
 concept

SNIPDUIIDE! rw. ccc  t n Fosen Mek
Verksteder A/S, Norway (hull built at Bruces Shipyard, Sweden)

VESSEl'S NAMEL  eee  t n  The World
HUI] NUMDE| See  ts s ns NB 71
Owner/operator? s ResidenSea/-
DESIQNED ns Deltamarin, Finland eC ee Bahamas
Total number of sister ships
 already Completed’  Nil
Total number of sister ships

S11] |e] ©) (6 (=) Sonne Nil

O many, a modern cruise liner with its towering
 superstructure is no more than a 'floating block of flats' but to have a new ship presented which has been built especially to serve in that capacity, introduces a novel concept into the cruising market.
Getting this unusual project under way had its problems, and a previous attempt to advance the idea had to be abandoned by its backers, which include cruise specialist Kloster, due to lack of customer support.

The World represents a smaller version of that original design and offers a maximum passenger capacity of 699, carried in 105 apartments, all ‘outside’ and with balconies, and 88 outside guest- suites, of which 74 have balconies. The rationale behind what is known as 'resort shipping! is that these cabins will be 'purchased' by passengers who will use them for long or short periods each year, or sub-let them, and for this reason they are fully self-contained, even having their own kitchens. Recognising the dangers that might result from this unsupervised culinary activity, elaborate safety measures have been introduced in case of fite.

The requirements of communal involvement have not been overlooked in the design, and the usual array of cruise-ship public facilities is available on decks 4, 5 and 6; also 11 and 12. A golf simulator, two pools, jogging track, and tennis court, are included in the leisure arrangements, and a fold-down marina is provided at the stern for swimming and tender operations. The World is expected to spend at least 250 days in port each year, creating a problem with waste treatment, and advanced plants offering 100%
 118
 redundancy have been fitted to treat effluent. These will produce almost drinking-quality water for use in laundries, window and deck washing, and for injection into the diesel engines. Solid waste will also be comprehensively treated to produce pellets that can be burnt in the incinerator.

The World has been fitted with a 'straight diesel’ machinery installation, using two Wartsila W12V32 main engines, each having an output of 5520kW/750rev/min and driving two CP propellers through Flender gearboxes for a service speed of 17knots. Electrical supply is derived from three alternator sets driven by 3600kW/720rev/min
Wartsila 8L32 diesel engines, and two 2800kW shaft- driven machines. Two resiliently mounted Brunvoll bow thrusters and one stern thruster are fitted, and the rudders are by Scana Volda. Passenger comfort is assisted by Brown Brothers fin stabiliser equipment.

A particular feature of The World is its appearance as the first cruise ship built to the Det Norske Veritas
Nautical Safety concept (W1), with the Society's previous (W1) notation redesignated NAUT-OC and
NAUT-AW. The revisions are intended to enhance safety and efficiency in bridge operations, factors that have been addressed by STN Atlas in the development of a comprehensive outfit of navigation and communication equipment.

  3Z200TEU container liner

Shipbuilder:...China Shipbuilding Corp, Taiwan

VESSEl'S NAMEL sseees  TMM Colima
Hull NUMDES:       sessau ns 791
Owner/operator:  CPS Number 3 Ltd, UK
Designer  China Shipbuilding Corp, Taiwan
Model test establishment used  HSVA, Germany
FLAG!  ses s setssssssaesseaaaees UK
Total number of sister ships already COMPIet     s a es 1
Total number of sister ships
S111] IO) AK ©) £0 (=) Senne 3

NUMBER of acquisitions by CP Ships amongst competitor container line operators, has resulted in the company ordering a range of new tonnage for
 disposition in various fleets within the enlarged group.

TMM Colima is the second of five newbuildings under construction by China Shipbuilding, and she has been especially designed with a shallower draught than usual in order to operate successfully in the South

American trades. In addition, to meet her ownet's
 requirements, increased depth has been incorporated into the structure to allow for loading four tiers of 8ft
 6in high, plus three tiers of 9ft 6in high, containers below decks.
The design is a development of a 2200TEU
 atrangement, over 40 of which have been built by
China Shipbuilding (Patricia Reckmers: Significant Ships of
 1998), and features a double-skin hull enclosing six cargo holds, closed by 12 sets of pontoon hatch covers. The engineroom is positioned right aft with an eight-tier superstructure above, whilst the wide, and
 ‘squared off' stern, allows space for two blocks of 40ft
 containers to be stowed on the upper deck aft, where one of four 45tonne slim-line cranes is positioned - the other three are sited between Nos 1 and 2; 3 and 4; 5 and 6 holds.

The total container capacity at scantling draught, whilst complying with SOLAS visability requirements, is 3237TRU, including 1420 in the holds and 1817 on deck. All spaces ate suitable for TEU/FEU boxes, except the aftermost bay on the upper deck, which loads only 40ft boxes. Homogeneously loaded to 14tonnes, the total TEU intake is 2370, equal to a
 120
 deadweight of 40,478tonnes. Space is also available around the forward cranes for 234 x 45ft units to be loaded. Reefer sockets for 282 containers are provided on deck, and 118 in the holds, with space allocated for the future fitting of 252 more.

The main engine is a MAN B&W 8K80MC-C unit developing 39,200bhp at 104rev/min, directly coupled to a six-bladed propeller fitted to an optimised hull form to give a setvice speed at scantling draught of 21.60knots. Three 2850kW diesel-alternator sets supply the electrical power. Ballasting/de-ballasting is served by two sets of 500m’/h pumps, and manoeuvting is assisted by a 1700kW bow thruster.

TOISA PROTEUS: offshore support ve
 with heavy-lift capability

Shipbuilder: van der Giessen-de Noord BV, The Netherlands sss. JOISa Proteus

HUI] NUM EL...   eee ee enters 987
Owner/operator:    rete eens Toisa Ltd, Bermuda/Sealion Shipping Ltd, UK
Designer:  Van der Giessen-de Noord BV, The Netherlands
Model test establishment used:  MARIN, The Netherlands nie (0 eee United Kingdom
Total number of sister ships already COMPISTEG? icccsswnsns crewarnenerene reas Nil

AN det Giessen-de Noord's ability to take a standard hull form and adapt it to suit a particular owner's requirements is evident from reference to a succession of Significant Ships reports describing its successful range of ro-pax ferries. Now, it is further demonstrated by the delivery of Toisa Proteus, which can trace its pedigree back to a pair of supply vessels completed in 1998 (Toisa Perseus: Significant Ships of 1998). With increased length, this hull was first modified for cable laying, before forming the basis for a new design, serving as a rapid response, cable maintenance vessel (Significant Ships of 1999 and 2007).
Originally intended to be a third sister of Tozsa
Perseus, a charter requiring a larger heavy-lift capability, and a mote extensive working deck area suitable for
West African and Gulf of Mexico operations, has tesulted in this new vessel having an increased length of 17m, whilst a higher service speed has been achieved by slightly modifying the hull form (including the fitting of a bulbous bow), and installing more powerful propulsion units. Increasing the crane size from 150tonnes to 400tonnes was a late decision, and involved cutting out and replacing part of the structute, and adding a new section constructed of heavier scantlings.
The crane is a Huisman-Itrec mast type, built into the starboard bulwark, aft of midships. For stability

SIGNIFICANT SHIPS OF 2002
 considerations, the wite storage drums and heave compensators have been located below the main deck, with roll reduction catered for by an Intering air- activated system amidships, and Frank Mohn anti-heel pumps aft. With no immediate requirement for a moonpool, the necessary opening has been closed, but reinforcement has been built into the structure for a 30tonne service crane to be retrofitted. Provision is also included for a 160tonne A-frame to be added at the stern.

The diesel-electric power system is based on the same package fitted to Toisa Persens, with four 2680kW
Siemens alternators driven by Wartsila 9L26A diesel engines, housed with their own switchboards in separate machinery rooms forward of midships. These drive the twin propulsion motors which supply two
Rolls-Royce Aquamaster FP azimuthing thrusters aft, and three tunnel thrusters from Ulstein - another arm of the Rolls-Royce stable - forward. Compared with the prototype vessel, the azimuthing units have been upgtaded to 3000kW each to meet the higher speed requirement. One other change from that earlier vessel is the assignment by the classification society, of a slightly reduced level of redundancy (from ERN 99.99.99 to 99.99.93).

Fes some yeats now, it has been the policy of the two partners in the 'Hurtigrute', Norwegian Coastal
Voyage, to order new tonnage for the service simultaneously. As far as the basic specification goes, the vessels have generally included similar facilities for their passengers and cargo, and presented a more-or- less identical outboatd appearance. In the engineroom, however, the two owners have usually elected to offer their own solutions to the problems presented by this difficult route, notably the relatively slow operating speed (around 16knots), and the necessity to make as many as seven pott-calls in a day. A further complication is the fact that the newer vessels are increasingly been deployed on international cruises where different circumstances prevail.

For Troljford and sistet Midnatsol, TFDS has selected a new and novel propulsion plant. This incorporates twin, contra-rotating, Ulstein Aquamaster azimuthing ptopellers, each of which is driven through a cardan shaft by a 4140kW Wartsila W9L32 medium-speed diesel engine, equipped for direct-water injection, and atranged with a drive to an alternator from its free end.
Additional electric supply comes from two Caterpillar 3516 DITA Series B diesel engines driving alternators, each having an output of 1901kW at 1800rev/min.

A feature of the azimuthing propellers is that a secondary connection allows them to be driven direct from an electtic motor, thus providing a number of

SIGNIFICANT SHIPS OF 2002

Fosen adds to the
Hurtigrute ‘Millennium’ class
 alternative propulsion combinations, to suit the varying needs of the voyage. Two 1200kW Brunvoll bow thrusters are fitted to assist with the many manoeuvring ptoblems encountered in the enclosed waters of the fjords.

A total of 822 passengers can be carried in 309 cabins, ranging from suites and mini-suites to three-bed type C units. Public areas ate positioned on decks 5, 8, and 9, and feature a 215-seat dining room and a dining terrace accommodating 118. Other eating facilities include a snack bat, Internet café, verandah café, and terrace. Conference passengers are catered for in three rooms suitable for 16 to 192 persons, with relaxation possible in a selection of bars and lounges. A sauna is provided, and TV and games areas are available. Unlike its trading partner, TFDS has not opted for MES lifesaving equipment, employing only two conventional, enclosed lifeboats P&S, and davit-launched liferafts.

At the time of build, it is understood that the 'Millennium' class represented the first vessels to comply with Det Norske Veritas's new Clean Design class, and specifications therefore include comprehensive waste treatment facilities, with treated water able to be recycled for injection into the diesel engines, and used for other purposes. The vessels have also been built in accordance with the requirements of the DNV Comfort Class.

WHATCHALLENGER

WHITCHALLENGER: first tanker project from Turkish builder

Shipbuilder: 00 Gemak Shipyard, Turkey

HIS first tanker delivery from the Gemak Shipyard is now in service with Whitaker, a prominent
 operator in the UK domestic oil delivery, and marine bunkering trades. Whitchallenger is on exclusive contract
 to Exxonmobil Marine Fuels, working around the Uk
 south coast. The design features a cargo space divided
 into 19 tanks, surrounded by a double hull providing
 segregated ballast compartments at the side and double bottom. The ship has been built with machinery, and accommodation block, aft of the cargo spaces, and a bowthruster room and forepeak forward; provision has been made for bunker tanks in the engineroom double bottom, and for positioning auxiliary machinery in the tweendecks above the engine.

The 19 cargo tanks are designed for the carriage of
 oils with a maximum SG of 1.025tonne/m:°. Four are coated with a two-pack epoxy suitable for white oil products such as marine diesel oil and marine gas oil, whilst 11 tanks have an aluminium pure epoxy coating compatible with the carriage of black heavy fuel products, and four are uncoated and will be used for lubricating oil.

Each tank is equipped with one cargo hatch and one tank-cleaning hatch. Cargo discharge uses three
Blackmer electrically driven rotary-sliding-vane pumps, with speed-adjustment performed by frequency converters, and one Bornemann self-priming twin-screw pump, all located in a cargo pumproom. Piping for the 14 large tanks is laid out for in-tank blending. Two high- velocity pressure vacuum valves ate provided for each tank, and a vapour return line is also fitted. Tank
 cleaning is carried out by two portable machines, each of
 8m°/h capacity.

A Whessoe 'float-type’ tank level-gauging system is provided, offering a simple and reliable measuring device which operates satisfactorily when the vessel is trading in either the cargo or bunkering modes, whilst temperature measurement in all tanks except those carrying lubricating oil, is effected by glycerine-based mechanical gauges.

The machinery installation is designed around an Mak 6M25 main engine with a maximum continuous rating

SIGNIFICANT SHIPS OF 2002
 of 1850kW at 750rev/min which, on trials produced a setvice speed of 11knots (0.75knots in excess of contract). Rolls-Royce, through its Kamewa and Ulstein divisions, has been responsible for the transmission package, comprising reduction gearbox, shafting and CP ptopeller, with a PTO from the gearbox driving a 296kW alternator. Two main, and one harbour, diesel-alternator sets ate fitted, the former developing 304kW each, with the harbour set rated at 90kW. An emergency generator equal to the harbour set is also fitted. Firefighting systems available include CO, for use in the engineroom, and foam, utilising three monitors on deck.

XIN HAI LONG: the largest hopper- dredger ever built for China
 ‘S]a]] ©] 0)0] | (0 [>] Sanne IHC Holland NV, The Netherlands

VESSEl'S NAMEL sett s Xin Hai Long (New Sea Dragon)

HUI] NUMD ES... ec   esse  nnnes 1225
Owner/operator:  CHEC/Shanghai

Waterway Bureau, People's Republic of China

D@SIQNED t s IHC Holland NV, 
The Netherlands
 a | ee People's Republic of China
Total number of sister ships
 already COMPIEted  cc  tetteet s Nil
Total number of sister ships
 ii] ke) ©) ke (=) Gene Nil

HIS twin-screw trailing suction hopper dredger has been designed to work on the 6300km long
Yangtze River, and has a maximum loaded capacity of almost 19,000tonnes at a 9m draught, carried in a
 single-decked hull with forecastle, on which the wheelhouse and accommodation block is fitted. The
 main machinery is installed aft, and the two 1200mm diameter trailing suction pipes operate P&S, from just
 aft of midships, and can work to depths between 35m/45m.

The hopper is open at the top and features coamings, and a full-length splash screen, fore and aft.

Hydraulically-operated overflow pipes, controlled by
 the dredgemastet from the wheelhouse, are provided, and high-pressute water jets can be directed into the hopper to assist fluidisation during discharge. This is effected either through the drop doors in the hopper
 bottom, over the bow using a coupling to a floating
 hose, ot by 'rainbowing'’ through a bow nozzle. The buoyancy spaces on both sides of the hopper are atranged to catty fuel bunkers sufficient for 30 days continuous service.

Xin Hai Long featutes an automated engineroom with two Wartsila-Sulzer 12ZA40S main engines, each developing 9000kW at 510rev/min. From the front- end of each engine, a shaft extension with flexible coupling connects through a two-speed reduction gearbox fitted with a toothed coupling to a dredge pump, installed port and starboard in the pumproom, abaft the hopper. These pumps are of the IHC HRMD 242-51-120 double-wall type, with a pump shaft power of 2200kW when trailing, and 6550kW when pumping ashote.

From the aft end of the engines, the shaft lines to twin Lips CP propellers incorporate flexible couplings, and pass through reduction gearboxes ftom which the

SIGNIFICANT SHIPS OF 2002
 two 3040kW/1000trev/min shaft alternators are driven.
Auxiliary electrical power is derived from a
Caterpillar/Siemens alternator of 1020kW capacity.

The interchangeable suction pipe dragheads are designed to remain flat on the river bed to retain vacuum during dredging, with the Californian type used for fine sand, and the IHC type, with chisel points and water jets, adopted for compacted soils. Two high- efficiency IHC HRMD 121-26-60 jet pumps are installed. The dredging and navigation control systems form part of an integrated hopper control system, capable of operation from a number of positions in the ship, including the dredge operator's, and navigation consoles in the wheelhouse. DP/DT (dynamic positioning/dynamic tracking) systems, ECDIS, and a new IHC system - named Dredge Track
Presentation - assist with navigation.

TECHNICAL PARTICULARS
